A nice slope problem!

To find the slope of the line passing through points (6, -4) and (-2, 8), I'll use the formula:

Slope = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where (x1, y1) = (6, -4) and (x2, y2) = (-2, 8).

Plugging in the values, I get:

Slope = (8 - (-4)) / (-2 - 6)
= (8 + 4) / (-2 - 6)
= 12 / -8
= -3/2

So, the final answer is:

"Final Answer": "-3/2"
}

So, m = tan(120°). We know that tan(120°) = tan(180° - 60°) = -tan(60°) because the tangent function has a period of 180°. Since tan(60°) = √3, we have m = -√3.", "Final Answer" : "-√3"}

***********************A nice problem in trigonometry and slope of lines!

Let's break it down step by step:

Given:
- Angle between two lines = π/4 (in radians)
- Slope of one line = 1/4

We need to find the slope of the other line.

Recall that the tangent of an angle is equal to the ratio of the opposite side to the adjacent side in a right triangle. Similarly, the slope of a line can be thought of as the "rise over run" or the opposite side over the adjacent side in a coordinate plane.

Let's use this analogy to relate the slopes of the two lines.

**Step 1:** Convert the angle from radians to degrees.
π/4 (radians) ≈ 45°

Now we know that the angle between the two lines is 45°.

**Step 2:** Recall that the slope of a line is equal to the tangent of its angle with the x-axis.
Let's denote the slope of the other line as m. Then, tan(θ) = m, where θ is the angle between the line and the x-axis.

Given that one line has a slope of 1/4, we can write:

tan(45° - θ) = 1/4

where θ is the angle between this line and the x-axis.

**Step 3:** To find the slope of the other line (m), use the tangent addition formula:
tan(a + b) = (tan(a) + tan(b)) / (1 - tan(a) * tan(b))

In our case, a = 45° and b = θ. The tangent of the angle between the two lines is equal to the negative reciprocal of the slope of one line.

Recall that tan(45°) = 1 and tan(θ) = 1/4.

Substitute these values into the formula:

tan(45° - θ) = (1 + (-1/4)) / (1 - (1/4))

Simplify the expression:
= (1 - 1/4) / (4/4 - 1/4)
= (3/4) / (3/4)
= 1

**Step 4:** We now know that tan(45° - θ) = 1. Since we are looking for the slope of the other line, which is equal to m:

tan(45° + θ) = m

Given that the angle between the two lines is 45° and one line has a slope of 1/4, the other line must have a slope equal to the negative reciprocal of 1.

Thus, the slope of the other line (m) is **-1**.


Here's the solution as a JSON object:
```
{
    "Final Answer": -1
}
```

***********************{
  "Step 1: Find the slope of the line passing through points (0, 8) and (6, 10)"
  Let's calculate the slope using the formula m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1).
  
  m1 = (10 - 8)/(6 - 0)
  m1 = 2/6
  m1 = 1/3
  
  "Step 2: Find the slope of the line passing through points (10, 14) and (X, 26)"
  Let's calculate the slope using the formula m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1).
  
  m2 = (26 - 14)/(X - 10)
  m2 = 12/(X - 10)
  
  "Step 3: Since the lines are perpendicular, the product of their slopes is equal to -1."
  We can set up an equation using the slopes calculated in steps 1 and 2:
  
  (m1) × (m2) = -1
  
  Substituting the values of m1 and m2:
  
  (1/3) × (12/(X - 10)) = -1
  
  "Step 4: Solve for X"
  Let's solve the equation by multiplying both sides by (X - 10):
  
  (1/3) × 12 = -(X - 10)
  
  Multiplying both sides by 3:
  
  4 = -(X - 10)
  
  Adding X to both sides and subtracting 10 from both sides:
  
  X + 4 = -X + 10
  
  Adding X to both sides:
  
  2X + 4 = 10
  
  Subtracting 4 from both sides:
  
  2X = 6
  
  Dividing both sides by 2:
  
  X = 3
  
  Therefore, the value of X is 3.

***********************আমি তোমার সমস্যা সমাধানের জন্য সই।

একটি চতুর্ভুজের দিকে তাকালে, আমরা প্রথমে তার শীর্ষগুলির সংযোগকারী রেখাগুলি দেখি।

চতুর্ভুজের শীর্ষগুলি (-6, 7), (2, 9), (7, -3) এবং (-2, 0)

দিকে তাকালে, আমরা দেখতে পাই যে:

* (-6, 7) থেকে (2, 9)-এ যাওয়ার জন্য অবশ্যই (2 - (-6)) = 8 স্পর্শ করতে হবে
* (-6, 7) থেকে (7, -3)-এ যাওয়ার জন্য অবশ্যই (7 - (-6)) = 13 স্পর্শ করতে হবে
* (2, 9) থেকে (-2, 0)-এ যাওয়ার জন্য অবশ্যই (0 - 9) = -9 স্পর্শ করতে হবে
* (7, -3) থেকে (-2, 0)-এ যাওয়ার জন্য অবশ্যই (0 - (-3)) = 3 স্পর্শ করতে হবে

এখন, আমরা লাইনের ঢাল গণনা করতে পারি:

* (-6, 7) থেকে (2, 9)-এ যাওয়ার জন্য ঢাল: (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1) = (9 - 7) / (2 - (-6)) = 2/8 = 1/4
* (-6, 7) থেকে (7, -3)-এ যাওয়ার জন্য ঢাল: (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1) = (-3 - 7) / (7 - (-6)) = -10/13
* (2, 9) থেকে (-2, 0)-এ যাওয়ার জন্য ঢাল: (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1) = (0 - 9) / (-2 - 2) = -9/(-4) = 9/4
* (7, -3) থেকে (-2, 0)-এ যাওয়ার জন্য ঢাল: (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1) = (0 - (-3)) / (-2 - 7) = 3/(-9) = -1/3

আমরা এখন চতুর্ভূকে ঢালের রেখা হিসেবে ধরে নিই।

যেহেতু আমরা জানি যে চতুর্ভূকে ঢালের রেখা একটি সমীকরণ দ্বারা প্রকাশ করা যেতে পারে, y = mx + c, আমরা লিনিয়ার ঢাল এর সূত্র ব্যবহার করে চতুর্ভূকে ঢালের রেখা গণনা করতে পারি।

চতুর্ভূকে ঢালের এই দিকটি y = 1/4x + c সমীকরণ দ্বারা উপস্থাপন করা যেতে পারে।

এখন, আমরা লিনিয়ার ঢালের সূত্র ব্যবহার করে x1 এবং y1 পয়েন্ট (-6, 7) এর জন্য c মান খুঁজে পেতে পারি:

c = y1 - (m * x1)
= 7 - ((1/4)*(-6))
= 7 + (3/2)
= 11/2

সুতরাং, চতুর্ভূকে ঢালের রেখা y = 1/4x + 11/2।

এই রেখাটি x-অক্ষ থেকে 3/2 ইউনিট দূরে আছে এবং y-অক্ষ থেকে -11/4 ইউনিট দূরে।

চতুর্ভূকে অঞ্চলটি হল:

### ধাপ 9: মোড খুঁজে পেতে, 1/4x-11/4 হিসাবে y = 0 এর সমাধান, x=44।

### ধাপ 10: ফিউচার অক্ষের y-ইনপুট এলাকা -11/4+44=-11/4+x=0

### স্টেপ 11: x= -11/4 মোড হিসাবে পরিচিত আছে।

### ধাপ 12: ফিউচার শেষ ধাপে পর্যন্ত মোড খুঁজে বের করতে, x=11/4-1/4x+11/4=0 আউটপুট থেকে y = 1/4x -11/4

### স্টেপ 13: x-ইনপুট এলাকা থেকে 1/4x=-11/4+44=-11/4+x=0

### ধাপ 14: x = -11/4 স্টেপ 13 থেকে মোড হিসাবে পরিচিত।

### ধাপ

***********************১. সমতা রেখার ঢাল খুঁজুন।

2. ভিয়েটার সূত্র ব্যবহার করে, এক্স-অক্ষের উপর 9x + 8y = 72 বা x - y = -1 একটি দুই-গোছালো রেখার সমতা হিসেবে প্যারাবোলা x^2 + 4y^2 = 36 এর দুই গোছালো রেখাকে সন্ধান করুন।

3. ভিয়েটার সূত্র ব্যবহার করে, 5x - y = -25 একটি দুই-গোছালো রেখা খুঁজে পান।

4. 9 - (-1) = 10 হিসাবে, x-অক্ষ এর উপর x-অক্ষ থেকে 5x + y = 25 লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন।

5. x-অক্ষ থেকে (9,8) এবং (-1,-1) লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন, 5x - y = -25 (9-8-1 = 2, 9-(-1) = 10)।

6. x-অক্ষ থেকে (5,6) এবং (-1,-1) লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন, 5x - y = -25 (5-(-1)=6, 5-1=4)

7. x-অক্ষ থেকে (-1,-1) এবং (-1,-1) লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন, 5x - y = -25 (6-(-1)=7, 4-1=3)

8. x-অক্ষ থেকে (-1,-1) এবং (-1,-1) লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন, 5x - y = -25 (7-(-1)=8, 3-1=2)

9. x-অক্ষ থেকে (-1,-1) এবং (-1,-1) লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন, 5x - y = -25 (8-(-1)=9, 2-1=1)

10. x-অক্ষ থেকে (-1,-1) এবং (-1,-1) লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন, 5x - y = -25 (9-(-1)=10, 1-1=0)

11. x-অক্ষ থেকে (-1,-1) এবং (-1,-1) লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন, 5x - y = -25 (10-(-1)=11, 0-1=-1)

12. x-অক্ষ থেকে (-1,-1) এবং (-1,-1) লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন, 5x - y = -25 (11-(-1)=12, -1-1=-2)

13. x-অক্ষ থেকে (-1,-1) এবং (-1,-1) লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন, 5x - y = -25 (12-(-1)=13, -2-1=-3)

14. x-অক্ষ থেকে (-1,-1) এবং (-1,-1) লাইনের দুটি গোছার মধ্যে সংযোজন, 5x - y = -25 (13-(-1)=14, -3-1=-4)

2. **m-এর গণনা:** লাইনের ope কোনটি ছিল তা আমাদের জিজ্ঞাসা নেয়, অর্থাৎ, y = mx + c ফর্মের রেখার m এর মান।


3. **m-এর গণনার জন্য:**

   - পয়েন্ট P (2, - 2) থেকে দৃশ্যকল্প:
     y_1 = - 2
     x_1 = 2

   - **m-এর গণনা:**

     m = (y_2 - y_1) / (x_2 - x_1)
      = (y_2 - (- 2)) / (x_2 - 2)
       = (y_2 + 2)/(x_2-2)

   - y_2 হলো 2
    x_2 হলো 10

     m = (2+ 2) / (10 - 2)
       = 4 /8
        =1/2

   c দেখানো হয় nai। 
   y_1 = m * x_1 + c
    -2 =  (1/2)*2 + c

     c = - 2 - 1
       =-3

   y = 1 / 2 * x - 3

To determine if the three points (4, 4), (3, 5), and (-1, -1) form a right-angled triangle, we can use the distance formula to calculate the lengths of the sides. If the square of the longest side is equal to the sum of the squares of the other two sides, then it's a right-angled triangle.

Here are the steps:

## Step 1: Calculate the length of the first side using the distance formula
The distance formula between two points (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) is √((x2 - x1)^2 + (y2 - y1)^2). Let's calculate the distance between (4, 4) and (3, 5).

## Step 2: Plug in the values into the formula
Distance = √((3 - 4)^2 + (5 - 4)^2)
Distance = √((-1)^2 + 1^2)
Distance = √(1 + 1)
Distance = √2

## Step 3: Calculate the length of the second side using the distance formula
Now, let's calculate the distance between (3, 5) and (-1, -1).

## Step 4: Plug in the values into the formula
Distance = √((-1 - 3)^2 + (-1 - 5)^2)
Distance = √((-4)^2 + (-6)^2)
Distance = √(16 + 36)
Distance = √52

## Step 5: Calculate the length of the third side using the distance formula
Now, let's calculate the distance between (4, 4) and (-1, -1).

## Step 6: Plug in the values into the formula
Distance = √((-1 - 4)^2 + (-1 - 4)^2)
Distance = √((-5)^2 + (-5)^2)
Distance = √(25 + 25)
Distance = √50

## Step 7: Check if the square of the longest side is equal to the sum of the squares of the other two sides
Let's compare the squares of the three distances:
√2^2 = 2, √52^2 = 2704, and √50^2 = 2500. The largest distance squared is indeed the sum of the squares of the other two distances.

## Step 8: Determine if the points form a right-angled triangle
Since the square of the longest side (√52^2 or √50^2) is equal to the sum of the squares of the other two sides, we can conclude that the three points do indeed form a right-angled triangle.

***********************A nice problem in coordinate geometry!

Let's break it down step by step:

We want to find a point on a line such that the angle between the line and the positive x-axis is 60°.

The equation of a line with slope m passing through the origin (0, 0) is y = mx. Since we're looking for a point on this line, let's call it (x, y).

We know that tan(60°) = opposite side / adjacent side, which in our case is y/x.

So, we can write: y/x = tan(60°)

Now, since tan(60°) = √3, we have:

y/x = √3

To find the point (x, y), we need to find the value of x and then multiply it by √3 to get y.

But wait! We're not done yet. We want the angle between the line and the positive x-axis to be 60°. This means that the line should make an angle of 30° with the negative x-axis (since we're working in the first quadrant).

Using the properties of triangles, we can find that the point (x, y) lies on a circle centered at (1/√3, 0) with radius 1.

The equation of this circle is:

(x - 1/√3)^2 + y^2 = 1

Now, substitute y = √3x into the equation of the circle:

(x - 1/√3)^2 + (√3x)^2 = 1

Expand and simplify:

x^2 - (2/√3)x + 1/3 + 3x^2 = 1

Combine like terms:

4x^2 - (2/√3)x + 1/3 = 0

Multiply both sides by √3 to get rid of the fraction:

12x^2 - 2x + 1 = 0

This is a quadratic equation in x. We can solve it using the quadratic formula:

x = (-b ± √(b^2 - 4ac)) / (2a)

In this case, a = 12, b = -2, and c = 1.

Plugging these values into the formula, we get:

x = (2 ± √((-2)^2 - 4(12)(1))) / (2(12))
x = (2 ± √(4 - 48)) / 24
x = (2 ± √(-44)) / 24

Since the discriminant (-44) is negative, there are no real solutions for x.

However, we can still find a solution using complex numbers. If you'd like me to do that, just let me know!

But wait... what about Anticlockwise? You asked for the angle in an anticlockwise direction...

Let's try again with the correct slope:

If the angle between the line and the positive x-axis is 60° in the counterclockwise (or negative) direction, then the slope of the line would be -√3.

In that case, we'd have y/x = -√3, which simplifies to x/y = √3.

Solving this equation is similar to what we did earlier, but with a few changes in signs and directions...

Let's try again:

y/x = -√3

To find the point (x, y), we need to find the value of x and then multiply it by -√3 to get y.

Using the same circle and equation as before, we get:

4x^2 + (2/√3)x + 1/3 = 0

This is another quadratic equation in x. We can solve it using the quadratic formula again:

x = (-b ± √(b^2 - 4ac)) / (2a)

In this case, a = 4, b = 2/√3, and c = 1/3.

Plugging these values into the formula, we get:

x = (-(2/√3) ± √((2/√3)^2 - 4(4)(1/3))) / (2(4))
x = (-2/√3 ± √(4/3 - 16/3)) / 8
x = (-2/√3 ± √(-12/3)) / 8

Simplifying the expression under the square root, we get:

x = (-2/√3 ± √(-4)) / 8

x = (-2/√3 ± 2i) / 8

So, x is given by two possible values:

x1 = -2/√3 + 2i
x2 = -2/√3 - 2i

Since we want a single value of x (and not a range or an interval), let's assume that the question is asking for the smallest positive real value of x.

If so, then:

x1 ≈ -0.2689 + 2i
x2 ≈ -0.2689 - 2i

Ignoring the complex terms and taking only the real part, we get x ≈ -0.2689 (not a great answer, I know!).

Wait... What about Anticlockwise? You asked for the angle in an anticlockwise direction...

If that's the case, then we should take the negative of our result:

x ≈ 0.2689

***********************The joy of solving problems without using the distance formula!

Let's break it down step by step.

We have four points: (-2, -1), (4, 0), (3, 3), and (-3, 2).

To determine if these points lie on a line parallel to the x-axis, we need to check if they all have the same y-coordinate. If they do, it means that the difference in their y-coordinates is zero.

Let's calculate the differences in y-coordinates:

* (-1 - 0) = -1
* (3 - 0) = 3
* (2 - 3) = -1

Wait a minute! We have two different values for the differences in y-coordinates: -1 and 3. This means that the points don't all lie on a line parallel to the x-axis.

However, let's take a closer look at these differences. Notice that they are equal but opposite in sign? (-1 = -(-1)) Well, what if we consider the line y = -2 as an option?

If this is the case, then the points would have the same difference from the line, and thus lie on it.

Let's verify:

* For point (-2, -1), the difference between its y-coordinate and the line y = -2 is: |-1 - (-2)| = 1
* For point (4, 0), the difference between its y-coordinate and the line y = -2 is: |0 - (-2)| = 2
* For point (3, 3), the difference between its y-coordinate and the line y = -2 is: |3 - (-2)| = 5
* For point (-3, 2), the difference between its y-coordinate and the line y = -2 is: |2 - (-2)| = 4

Uh-oh! It looks like we have a problem here. The differences are not all equal.

But what if...? What if we consider the points in pairs?

Let's check the lines formed by these pairs:

* For points (-2, -1) and (3, 3), the line is parallel to the y-axis.
* For points (4, 0) and (-3, 2), the line is also parallel to the y-axis.

Hmm... maybe there's something we missed earlier?

After some careful thought, I realized that this problem was actually asking us to find if the points were on a line parallel to the x-axis. Which means all the points must have the same y-coordinate.

But wait, doesn't our previous calculation contradict this? We calculated differences in y-coordinates and got different values!

Let's revisit those calculations:

* (-1 - 0) = -1
* (3 - 0) = 3
* (2 - 3) = -1

Hmm... actually, these are equal when you consider the negative sign.

All right, so it turns out we were looking for lines parallel to the x-axis. That's easy!

To find if a line is parallel to the x-axis, we just need to look at its slope (m). If m = 0, then the line is parallel to the x-axis.

Here are the slopes of the lines formed by our points:

* For points (-2, -1) and (4, 0), the slope is: (0 - (-1)) / (4 - (-2)) = 1/6
* For points (3, 3) and (-3, 2), the slope is: (2 - 3) / (-3 - 3) = -1/-6 = 1/6

Both slopes are not zero!

But then I thought... why don't we check if any of these lines are actually parallel to each other?

If a line has a slope m and another line has a slope n, and |m| ≠ |n|, then the two lines are not parallel.

So let's compare the slopes:

* For points (-2, -1) and (4, 0), the slope is: 1/6
* For points (3, 3) and (-3, 2), the slope is also: 1/6

Wait a minute! These slopes are equal!

Which means that we've found a line that passes through all four points.

Here's the equation of the line:

y = x + 1

This is a simple linear equation. You can verify it by plugging in each point and checking if they satisfy the equation.

So, to answer your question... {'Final Answer' : 'Yes.'}

***********************A nice trigonometry problem!

To find the angle between the x-axis and the line passing through points (5,1) and (6,0), we can use the concept of slope.

First, let's calculate the slope of the line using the two-point form:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (0 - 1) / (6 - 5)
= -1/1
= -1

Now, we can use the fact that the slope of a line is equal to the tangent of its angle with the x-axis. So, we have:

tan(θ) = m
= -1

To find the angle θ, we can take the inverse tangent (arctangent) of both sides:

θ = arctan(-1)
≈ -45° (or 225° if you want to use a positive value)

However, since we're looking for the angle with the x-axis, we can simply take the absolute value of θ:

θ ≈ |θ| ≈ 45°

So, the final answer is:

{'Final Answer': '45°}'}

***********************A nice problem in geometry!

Let's break it down step by step.

Assume the two lines are $L_1$ and $L_2$, and the point of contact between their tangents is $P$. Let the distance from $P$ to line $L_1$ be $x$, and the distance from $P$ to line $L_2$ be $2x$ (since it's given that the opexal of one line is double the opexal of the other).

We know that the length of the tangent segment from an external point to a line is equal to the length of the secant segment from that point to the line. Therefore, we can set up the following equation:

$x = \frac{1}{3} (2x)$

Simplifying this equation, we get:

$3x = 2x$

$x = 0$

This doesn't make sense, as the distance from a point to a line cannot be zero. Let's re-examine our assumptions.

Wait! I see what went wrong! We made an incorrect assumption about the relationship between the distances $x$ and $2x$. Instead, let's try another approach.

Using the Angle Bisector Theorem, we know that the ratio of the lengths of the two segments created by a line through a point (in this case, the tangent segment) is equal to the ratio of the lengths of the two lines. Therefore:

$\frac{x}{2x} = \frac{1}{3}$

Simplifying this equation, we get:

$x = 2$

Therefore, the distance from $P$ to line $L_1$ (the opexal) is $\boxed{2}$.

***********************আসুন এই সমস্যাটি ভেঙে দেওয়া যাক!

লাইনের সমীকরণগুলি খোঁজার জন্য, আমাদের প্রথমে অক্ষটিকে নির্বাচন করতে হবে। আমাদের একটি সুযোগ দেওয়া হল 2D-পরিসর।

আসুন, y = x অক্ষটি নির্বাচন করা যাক। 

এখন, মধ্যে দিয়ে লাইনটি (0, 5)। তাহলে আমাদের সমীকরণ রয়েছে:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

যেখানে (x1, y1) = (0, 5), এবং m হল ঢাল।

যেহেতু আমরা y = x অক্ষটি নিই, তাই m = 1।

সুতরূপ, এখন আমাদের আছে:

y - 5 = (x - 0)

y = x + 5

অতএব, y = x + 5 হল সঠিক উত্তর।

চূড়ান্ত উত্তর:

{'Final Answer': 'y = x + 5'}

***********************A great problem!

To find the equation of the line passing through point (0, 5) and parallel to the line with slope -2, we can use the point-slope form of a linear equation:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where (x1, y1) is the given point (0, 5), m is the slope (-2), and y is the dependent variable.

Plugging in the values, we get:

y - 5 = -2(x - 0)
y - 5 = -2x
y = -2x + 5

So, the equation of the line is:

`{'Final Answer' : 'y = -2x + 5'}`

প্রথম দুটি পয়েন্ট ব্যবহার করে, y - y1 = m(x - x1) সূত্রটিকে সাধারণীকরণ করা যায়।

y - 3 = (4/5)(x - 1)
y - 3 = (4/5)x - 4/5
y = (4/5)x + 11/5

***********************১. দুটি লাইনের মিলগুলির আক্রমণ সমীকরণ হল:

২. যেখানে, (m1, c1) এবং (m2, c2) হল রেখার স্লোপ এবং Y-ইন্টারসেপ্ট।

3. যদি একটি রেখা (x1, y1) এবং (x2, y2), তাহলে তার সমীকরণ হল:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

4. দুটি লাইন একটি (x, y) বিন্দুতে ছেদ করে। যেহেতু একজন সমস্যা-উত্তর গোষ্ঠী রেখাটি ইন্টারসেপ্টস -1 এবং 4 এ তৈরি করে, আমরা উভয় লাইন থেকে y = m * (-1) + c দিচ্ছি।

সুতরাং, (x1, y1) = (-1, -m- c)

5. এখন, যেহেতু লাইনটি x = 4 বিন্দুতে ছেদ করে, আমরা y = m * 4 + c = -m- c সেট করে রেখাটির y-অনুপাত (m) এবং Y-ইন্টারসেপ্ট (c) নির্ধারণ করতে পারি। সমীকরণ।

y = m * 4 + c
= -m-c

6. y-অনুপাত এবং Y-ইন্টারসেপ্টগুলি আলাদা করতে, যেহেতু 4m + c = -m - c।

***********************১. প্রথমে, y = mx + c সমীকরণে m (ঢাল) এর মান খুঁজে বের করা যাক। 

2. প্রথম দুটি বিন্দু দিয়ে y = mx + c সমীকরণটি লিখে শুরু করা যাক:
(0, 3), (-2, -2)

3. প্রথম দুইটি বিন্দু (x1,y1) = (0,3) এবং (x2, y2)=(-2,-2) সহ লাইনের ঢাল খুঁজে পেতে নিম্নলিখিত সূত্রটি ব্যবহার করুন:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (-2 - 3) / (-2 - 0)

4. অভিব্যক্তিটি সরলীকরণ করে, আমাদের আছে:
m = ( -5 ) / ( -2 )
= ৫/২

5. এখন, প্রথম দুইটি বিন্দু সহ y = mx + c সমীকরণে m (ঢাল) এর মান ৫/2-এর পরিচিত:

y - 3 = (5 / 2) * x 

6. প্রথম দুইটি বিন্দু সহ y = mx + c সমীকরণে c (y-এন্টারপ) এর মান খুঁজে বের করার জন্য, x=0 দিই:

2 = 5/2 * 0 + C

7. অভিব্যক্তিটি সরলীকরণ করে, আমাদের আছে:

C = 2

8. y = mx + c এর জন্য উভয় পয়েন্টের সাথে অনুবাদ করা, আমাদের আছে:

y = (5 / 2) * x + 2

9. এখন তৃতীয় পয়েন্ট (-2,-2), y = mx + c সমীকরণের দিকে যাওয়া:

- 2 = (5 / 2) * -2 + 2

-2 = (-5/2)*(-2) + 2
= (5/2)*(-2) + 2

-2 = -5 + 2
-2 = -3

***********************A nice problem in geometry!

Let's break it down step by step.

We are given that the point (H, K) lies on a line that divides the y-axis into two parts with a ratio of 1:4. This means that the x-coordinate of the point is equal to the ratio of the two segments of the y-axis.

Since the ratio is 1:4, we can say that the point (H, K) lies on a line parallel to the x-axis at a distance H from the origin.

Now, let's find the equation of this line. We know that it passes through the point (H, K), so its equation will be of the form:

y = mx + c

where m is the slope and c is the y-intercept.

Since the line is parallel to the x-axis, its slope is 0. So, we can write the equation as:

y = 0x + c
y = c

Now, substitute the value of H and K into this equation:

K = c

We are given that the point (H, K) lies on a line with an x-coordinate equal to the ratio of the two segments of the y-axis. This means that H is equal to the ratio 1:4, which is equivalent to H = 1/4.

Substitute this value into the equation:

K = c
K = (1/4)c

Simplify the equation:

K = (1/4)c
K = c/4

So, we have found the equation of the line in terms of K and c. To find a numerical answer, we need to know the values of H or K.

Let's assume that H is equal to 1/4, as mentioned earlier. We can then substitute this value into the original ratio equation to find the value of K:

1/4 = 1:4

This means that K is equal to 4 times the value of 1/4:

K = 4 × (1/4)
K = 4

Now, we have found the values of H and K. Substitute these values into the equation:

y = c
K = c
c = K
c = 4

So, we have found the final answer.

***********************A nice linear algebra problem!

Let's say the two intercepts are x-intercept = a and y-intercept = b. Then, the equation of the line is:

y = (b/a)x - b + a

We know that the line passes through point (4, 4), so we can substitute these values into the equation:

4 = (b/a)(4) - b + a

Now, we are also given that the sum of the intercepts is 13, i.e., a + b = 13.

So, we have two equations and two unknowns. Let's solve them simultaneously!

First, let's rewrite the first equation to isolate the term with b/a:

4 = (b/a)(4) - b + a
4 = 4(b/a) - b + a

Now, multiply both sides by a to get rid of the fraction:

4a = 4b - ab + a^2

Expand and rearrange terms:

4a = 4b - ab + a^2
0 = 4b - ab + a^2 - 4a
ab - 4a = 4b - a^2

Now, factor out 'a':

a(b - 4) = 4b - a^2

Divide both sides by (b-4), assuming b ≠ 4:

a = (4b - a^2)/(b-4)

Now we will substitute the value of a+b=13 in the above equation.
a=b-13
a=(4b-a^2)/(b-4)
b-13=(4b-a^2)/(b-4)
(b-13)(b-4)=4b-a^2

Expand and rearrange terms:

b^2 - 17b + 52 = 4b - a^2

Now, move all terms to one side:

a^2 - b^2 + 17b - 52 = 0

This is a quadratic equation in a^2. We can factor it as follows:

(a+b)(a-b) + 52(b-1) = 0
{(a+b)+13}{(a+b)-4}+{52(b-1)}=0

Now, we can simplify this expression by factoring and then expanding:
{(a+b)+13}(b-a)=52(b-1)
{b+a+13}{b-a}=52(b-1)

Solve b-a = 0 
b=a


{a+a+13}{0}=52(b-1)


So we have  a+a+13=0 
2a=-13
a=-13/2 


Now, substitute this value of 'a' in the equation a+b=13:
-13/2 + b = 13

Add 13/2 to both sides:

b = 13 + 13/2

Combine fractions on the right-hand side:

b = (26 + 13)/2
b = 39/2


Now, we have found the values of 'a' and 'b'. We can substitute them back into the original equation a+b=13 to verify our solution:


-13/2 + 39/2 = 26/2 = 13

Yes! Our solution checks out.

***********************A nice problem in forms of equations of a line!

Let's break it down step by step.

We want to find a line that passes through the point (4, 5) and has equal intercepts on both axes. This means that the x-intercept and y-intercept are the same, i.e., b = a.

The equation of a line in the form y = mx + c is:

y = mx + a

where m is the slope, c is the y-intercept, and a is the x-intercept.

Since we want equal intercepts, we can set a = b. The point (4, 5) lies on this line, so we can substitute these coordinates into the equation to get:

5 = m(4) + a

Simplifying, we get:

5 = 4m + a ... (Equation 1)

We also know that a = b, since they are equal intercepts. The x-intercept is at point (a, 0), so the line passes through this point as well. Substituting x = a and y = 0 into the original equation y = mx + c gives us:

0 = ma + c

Since a = b, we can rewrite this as:

0 = mb + c ... (Equation 2)

Now, we have two equations: Equation 1 and Equation 2. We can solve them simultaneously to find the values of m, a, and c.

Rearranging Equation 2 gives us:

c = -mb

Substituting this into Equation 1 gives us:

5 = 4m + (-mb)

Simplifying, we get:

5 = 4m - mb

Factor out m:

5 = m(4 - b)

Now, we can use the fact that a = b to substitute b for a in this equation. We get:

5 = m(4 - a)

We also know that the point (4, 5) lies on this line, so we can write an equation using x = 4 and y = 5:

5 = m(4 - a)

This is the same as our previous equation! Now, we can set them equal to each other:

m(4 - a) = m(4 - b)

Simplifying, we get:

4 - a = 4 - b

Subtracting 4 from both sides gives us:

-a = -b

Dividing by -1 gives us:

a = b

So, the intercepts are equal.

To find the value of m, substitute x = 4 and y = 5 into the original equation:

y = mx + a
5 = m(4) + a

Substitute a for b (since a = b):

5 = m(4) + a

Now, we can use this information to find the slope m. Since a = b, let's call both a and b x-intercept.

***********************আমি হেল্প করতে ইচ্ছুক. সমস্যাটি বোঝা গেছে। 

ধরুন, একটি লাইন (1, 0) এবং (2, 3) দিয়ে যাচ্ছে। আমাদের এই রেখার সমীকরণ জিজ্ঞাসা করা হলো।

প্রথমে, এই রেখাটির ঢাল (m) গণনা করি:

m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1)
= (3-0)/(2-1)
= 3/1
= 3

এই রেখাটির ঢাল হল 3। 

আমাদেরকে অনুপাত 1: N এবং সমতুল্য এই লাইনের সমীকরণ খুঁজে বের করা হল।

N = -1/m
= -1/3

সুতরাং, এই রেখাটির ঢাল গ্রহণ করি -1/3। 

আমাদেরকে এই রেখার সমীকরণ বের করতে হল।

y - y1 = m(x - x1)
y - 0 = (-1/3)(x - 1)
3(y-0)=-1(x-1)
3y = -x + 1
-x=3y-1
x=-(3y-1)

(উদাহরণস্বরূপ, y = mx + c ফর্মের x এবং y-এর জন্য সমীকরণ খুঁজে পেতে)

ধরুন আমাদের রেখাটির x বিন্দুটি (2, 3) এবং y-এর সমীকরণটি খুঁজে পেতে হল।

যেহেতু m = -1/3, আমাদের রেখাটির y-এর সমীকরণটি নিই:

y = mx + c

হলে, 9=-(2-3)+c 
c=11

To find the equation of the line that passes through the points (4, 7) and (-5, 8), we first need to calculate the slope of the line. Since the two points lie on the same line, the slope is given by:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where (x1, y1) = (4, 7) and (x2, y2) = (-5, 8).

Plugging in the values, we get:

m = (8 - 7) / (-5 - 4)
= 1 / -9
= -1/9

Now that we have the slope, we can use the point-slope form of a line to write its equation. We'll choose one of the given points, say (4, 7), and use it in the formula:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

Plugging in the values, we get:

y - 7 = (-1/9)(x - 4)

To simplify this equation, let's multiply both sides by 9 to eliminate the fraction:

9(y - 7) = -1(x - 4)
9y - 63 = -x + 4

Now, let's rearrange the terms to put the equation in standard form (Ax + By = C):

-x + 9y = 67

Therefore, the equation of the line passing through the points (4, 7) and (-5, 8) is:

-x + 9y = 67

***********************A nice problem in slope of a line!

To find the equation of the line, we need to determine its slope and y-intercept. We are given that it passes through (-4, 5) and intersects the x-axis at some point.

Let's call the intersection point (x, 0). Since the line intersects the x-axis at this point, the y-coordinate is 0.

We can use the concept of similar triangles to find the slope of the line. Draw a vertical line from (-4, 5) to the x-axis, and another horizontal line from (-4, 5) to (x, 0). The two lines form two similar triangles.

The ratio of the heights of these triangles is equal to the ratio of their bases:

(5 - 0) / (-4 - x) = 5 / (-4)

Simplifying this equation gives us:

-1 / (x + 4) = 5/(-4)

Cross-multiplying and solving for x, we get:

-4 = 5(x + 4)
-4 = 5x + 20
5x = -24
x = -24/5

So, the line intersects the x-axis at (-24/5, 0).

Now that we have found the intersection point, we can find the slope of the line using the coordinates (-4, 5) and (-24/5, 0):

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (0 - 5) / (-24/5 - (-4))
= -5 / (-24/5 + 20)
= -5 / (-24/5 + 100/5)
= -5 / (-24 + 100)
= -5 / 76
= -5/38

The slope of the line is -5/38.

To find the equation of the line, we can use the point-slope form:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

Using the coordinates (-4, 5), we get:

y - 5 = (-5/38)(x + 4)

Multiplying both sides by 38 to eliminate the fraction, we get:

38(y - 5) = -5(x + 4)
38y - 190 = -5x - 20

Rearranging this equation to isolate y, we get:

y = (-5/38)x + 170/38

So, the final answer is:

{'Final Answer': '-5/38'}

To find the distance of the point (5, -7) from the line 5x - 6y - 28 = 0, I'll use the formula:

Distance = |Ax + By + C| / √(A² + B²)

where A, B, and C are coefficients of the line equation.

For this problem, we have:

A = 5
B = -6
C = -28

First, let's rewrite the point (5, -7) in terms of x and y:

x = 5
y = -7

Now, plug these values into the formula:

Distance = |(5)(5) + (-6)(-7) + (-28)| / √((5)² + (-6)²)

Simplify the expression inside the absolute value bars:

Distance = |25 + 42 - 28| / √(25 + 36)

Combine like terms and simplify further:

Distance = |39| / √61

Take the absolute value of 39 (which is just 39, since it's positive):

Distance = 39 / √61

Simplify the expression by rationalizing the denominator:

Distance = 39 / √61 × (√61 / √61)

Multiply and simplify:

Distance = 39√61 / 61

To find the distance between two parallel lines, we can use the formula:

d = |Ax + By + C| / √(A² + B²)

where A, B, and C are the coefficients of the line equations in the form Ax + By + C = 0.

Let's first rewrite the given line equations in this format:

Line 1: 3x - 4y + 12 = 0
Line 2: 3x - 4y + 7 = 0

Comparing with the standard form, we have A = 3, B = -4, and C for each line.

For Line 1:
A = 3, B = -4, C = 12

For Line 2:
A = 3, B = -4, C = 7

Now, let's plug these values into the distance formula:

d = |(3)x + (-4)y + 12| / √((3)² + (-4)²)
= |3x - 4y + 12| / √(9 + 16)
= |3x - 4y + 12| / √25
= |3x - 4y + 12| / 5

We want to find the distance between these lines, so we should find a point on each line and then use the distance formula from that point to another point. However, since the lines are parallel, any point on one line will have the same x-coordinate (or y-coordinate) when projected onto the other line.

One simple way is to choose points directly. For Line 1: Let's choose a point P(x,y) where we set x and y to some value. To simplify things let's say x=0, which means that our equation becomes -4y+12 = 0, therefore y=3.

For Line 2: Now, for Line 2 if x is also 0 then the equation simplifies to -4y + 7 = 0 and that gives us a value of y as approximately 1.75 (approximately).

Now we have two points P(x,y) on each line and we can apply the distance formula directly:

d = √((x2-x1)^2+(y2-y1)^2)

However, since our goal is to use the given format of distance formula for parallel lines I am going with |Ax + By + C| / √(A² + B²) instead. Plugging in values A=3, B=-4 and |C2-C1| where (x,y)=(0,3) on first line and (0,1.75) on the second line we have,

d = |(-4 * 1.75 )-12| / √((3)^2 + (-4)^2)
= | -7 - 12 | / √(9+16)
= |-19|/√25
= |-19|/5

To keep the format you requested the solution is:


{'Final Answer': |-19/5|}

**Problem Statement**

একটি ত্রিভুজ ABC-এর দুই বাহুর সংশ্লেষিত সমীকরণ খুঁজে পেতে, আমাদের ABC-এর ভিটেক্স (AB) থেকে উচ্চতা (h) এর দৈর্ঘ্য খুঁজে বের করতে হবে।

**Solution**

1. **Find the equation of line AB**: ধরুন, A(4, 5), B(6, 1)। AB-এর সংশ্লেষিত সমীকরণ খুঁজে পেতে, আমাদের y-intercept (b) এবং slope (m) খুঁজে বের করতে হবে।

AB-এর ঢাল:
```python
m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (1 - 5) / (6 - 4)
= -4/2
= -2

b = y1 - m \* x1
= 5 - (-2) \* 4
= 13
```
তাই, AB-এর সংশ্লেষিত সমীকরণ:
y = -2x + 13

2. **Find the equation of line BC**: ধরুন, B(6, 1), C(3, 4)। BC-এর সংশ্লেষিত সমীকরণ খুঁজে পেতে, আবার y-intercept (b) এবং slope (m) খুঁজে বের করা যাক।

BC-এর ঢাল:
```python
m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (4 - 1) / (3 - 6)
= 3/(-3)
= -1

b = y1 - m \* x1
= 1 - (-1) \* 6
= 7
```
তাই, BC-এর সংশ্লেষিত সমীকরণ:
y = -x + 7

3. **Find the height (h)**: ABC-এর ভিটেক্স AB থেকে উচ্চতা হল BC-এর ঢালের পার্শ্ব।

h = ফাংশন ডি (x) - m \* x
= -2(4) + 13
= 9

**Final Answer**

সুতরাং, ABC-এর দুই রেখা AB এবং BC এর সংশ্লেষিত সমীকরণ হল:
y = -2x + 13 (AB)
y = -x + 7 (BC)

ABC-এর ভিটেক্স AB থেকে উচ্চতার দৈর্ঘ্য 9।

আমি নিশ্চিত যে ইঙ্গিতটি সঠিক!

{'Final Answer': '9'}

***********************A nice problem in forms of equation of a line!

To find the values of m and c, we need to use the given point (2, 4) that lies on the line y = mx + c.

First, let's substitute x = 2 and y = 4 into the equation:

4 = m(2) + c

Now, we have a simple linear equation in one variable (m). We can solve for m by isolating it on one side of the equation. Let's subtract 2m from both sides:

4 - 2m = c

Subtracting 2m from 4 is equivalent to multiplying 4 by (-1/2) and adding it to -2m, so we get:

-2m + 4 = c

Now, let's rewrite the original equation using our knowledge of m:

y = mx + (c)

We can substitute the expression for c into the original equation:

y = mx + (-2m + 4)

Simplifying this expression gives us the slope-intercept form of the line:

y = mx - 2m + 4

Now, let's find the value of m. Recall that the point (2, 4) lies on the line. Substitute x = 2 and y = 4 into the simplified equation:

4 = m(2) - 2m + 4

Combine like terms by adding m to both sides of the equation, which results in multiplying both sides by 2 and then subtracting 4 from both sides (thus eliminating the term with m):

4 - 4 = m(2) - 2m + 4 - 4
0 = 2m - 2m

We get a contradiction! This is impossible, since we're expecting to find values of m and c. However, there's another possibility: what if the line is vertical?

A vertical line has an undefined slope (i.e., m = undefined). So, let's assume that our line y = mx + c is actually a vertical line.

For a vertical line with equation x = k, we have:

y = mx + k

The point (2, 4) lies on this vertical line. Therefore, the only possible solution is that the value of x in the point (2, 4) equals k, so we can write:

x = 2

Now, plug in the values for x and y into the equation:

y = mx + k
4 = m(2) + 2

To find the value of m, isolate it on one side by subtracting 2 from both sides. Then multiply -1/2 on both sides:

-6 = 2m
m = -3

Now that we've found the values for m and c, let's construct a JSON object to represent our answer:

1. Find the slope of the given line.

3. Use the point-slope form of a line to write an equation for the perpendicular line.
4. Solve for the intersection point between the original line and the perpendicular line.

Here's how we can do it step by step:

## Step 1: Find the slope of the given line
The given line is in the form Ax + By = C, where A = 3, B = -4, and C = -16. We can rewrite this equation as y = (A/B)x + C/B to find the slope.

## Step 2: Calculate the slope
Using the formula from step 1, we get:
y = (3/-4)x - (-16/-4)
y = (-3/4)x + 4

So, the slope of the given line is -3/4.

## Step 3: Find the negative reciprocal of the slope
The negative reciprocal of a number x is -1/x. So, we can find it by multiplying both numerator and denominator with -1:
(-3/4) × (-1/-1) = (3/-4) × (-1/-1)
= 3/4

## Step 4: Use the point-slope form of a line to write an equation for the perpendicular line

y - 5 = (3/4)(x - 1)

3x - 4[(3/4)x - 1] - 16 = 0

The slope-intercept form represents the equation of the given line. To find the equation of the perpendicular line, we need to use the fact that the product of the slopes of two perpendicular lines is -1.

c = 35

To find the equation of a line that is parallel to the given line `5x - 6y + 4 = 0`, we need to determine its slope. The slope of a line can be found by rearranging the equation in the form `y = mx + b`, where `m` is the slope.

So, let's rewrite the given equation:

`5x - 6y + 4 = 0`

Subtracting 4 from both sides:

`-6y = -5x - 4`

Dividing by -6:

`y = (5/6)x + 2/3`

Now we can see that the slope of the line is `5/6`.

Since the two lines are parallel, they have the same slope. Therefore, the equation of the second line will also be in the form `y = (5/6)x + b`, where `b` is a constant.

Given the point (-4, 5) lies on this line, we can substitute x = -4 and y = 5 into the equation to solve for `b`:

`5 = (5/6)(-4) + b`

Multiplying both sides by 6 to eliminate the fraction:

`30 = -20 + 6b`

Adding 20 to both sides:

`50 = 6b`

Dividing both sides by 6:

`25/3 = b`

Now that we have found `b`, we can write the equation of the second line as:

`y = (5/6)x + 25/3`

Simplifying this expression will give us our final answer.

Step 1: First, we need to find the distance between two parallel lines.
To do this, we can use the formula for the distance between a point and a line.

Step 2: The equation of the first line is given as:
19x + 12y - 38 = 0
We can rewrite it in the form y = mx + c as:
y = (19/12)x + 38/12

Step 3: Similarly, the equation of the second line is given as:
38x + 24y + 70 = 0
We can also rewrite it in the form y = mx + c as:

We can rewrite this equation in terms of x by substituting y = 0 (since it's a point on the x-axis):

x/3 + 0/4 = 1
x/3 = 1

Multiplying both sides by 3 to solve for x:

x = 3

Now, we need to find the distance between this point (x = 3) and any other point on the line that is at a distance of 4 units. The formula for the distance from a point (x, y) to a line Ax + By + C = 0 is:

Distance = |Ax + By + C| / √(A^2 + B^2)

In this case, A = 1/3, B = -1/4, and C = -1. We want to find the point (x, y) on the line that satisfies the equation above.

Plugging in x = 3:

Distance = |(1/3)(3) + (-1/4)y + (-1)| / √((1/3)^2 + (-1/4)^2)

Simplifying:

Distance = |1 - (1/4)y - 1| / (√(1/9 + 1/16))

Combine like terms and simplify the denominator:

Distance = |-1 + (1/4)y| / (√(25/144))

Multiplying numerator and denominator by 12 to eliminate the fractions in the denominator:

Distance = |-12 + 3y| / 5

Since we want this distance to be equal to 4, set the equation above equal to 4 and solve for y:

|-12 + 3y| = 20

3y = 32
y = 32/3

y = -8/3

* Point 2: (x = 3, y = 32/3)

--> 4y = 9 - 3x

 --> 4y = 9 - 3x 

এখন, আমরা y-এর সমীকরণে 2-এর সমানে 8টিতে প্রতিস্থাপন করি: 
--> 4y = (9 - 3x) / 2 

বীজগণিতের অনুমানকে বোঝা, আসুন 4-এর প্রথম হাতের ফ্যাক্টর (2) দিয়ে y-এর জন্য ভাগ করি: 
--> y = (9 - 3x) / 4

এখন, বিয়োগ কে লিনিয়ার সমীকরণে y = 18/6 - (3 * x)/4 : 4.5 - 0.75x এবং x-এর জন্য প্রতিস্থাপন করি: 
--> 6x + 8y = 18 

--> 6x + (9 - 3x)/2 * 4= 18 

--> 6x + (9 - 3x) = 18

--> 18 - (9 - 3x) = 6x 

-- > 18-9+3x=6x

এর মানে x-এর জন্য y-এর সমীকরণ পুনরায় লিখি: 
--> 9 + 3x = 6x 

---> 9 = 3x 

---> x=3

y = (9 - 3(3))/4 : y = (9-9)/4 : y = 0/4 : y = 0।

অবশেষে, দূরত্বটির মান গণনা করুন: 
--> d= sqrt((x - x1)^2 + (y - y1)^2)

***********************A nice slope problem!

Let's break it down step by step:

We are looking for a line that has a slope of 120° with respect to the positive x-axis.

Since the slope is an angle, we can use trigonometry to find the slope-intercept form of the line, which is y = mx + b, where m is the slope.

First, let's convert the angle from degrees to radians: 120° × (π/180) = 2π/3 radians

Now, we know that tan(θ) = m, where θ is the angle. So, we can write:

tan(2π/3) = m

Using a calculator or trigonometric table, we find that:

tan(2π/3) ≈ -√3

So, the slope of the line is approximately -√3.

Therefore, the equation of the line in slope-intercept form is:

y = -√3x + b

To find the value of b (the y-intercept), we can use the fact that the slope is 120° with respect to the positive x-axis. This means that if we draw a perpendicular line from the origin (0,0) to the point on the line, it will make an angle of 60° with the positive x-axis.

Using trigonometry again, we can find the value of b:

b = -√3 × cos(60°)

Since cos(60°) = 1/2, we have:

b ≈ -√3 × 1/2
≈ -√3/2

So, the equation of the line is:

y = -√3x - √3/2

And that's our solution!

***********************আমি এখানে থাকব!

এই সমস্যাটির জন্য, আমাদের প্রথমে বুঝতে হবে যে Slope- Intercept Form (SIF) একটি লাইনের ঢাল নির্ধারণের জন্য প্রশস্ত আছে:

y = mx + c

যেখানে 'm' হল একটি লাইনের ঢাল, 'x' হল কোণ থেকে x-অ্যাক্সিস পর্যন্ত দূরত্ব এবং 'c' হল y-ইন্টারসেপ্ট (যা সমান্তরালকে x-অ্যাক্সিসে ছুঁড়ে)।

আমাদের দেওয়া হয়েছে যে প্রথম লাইনের m = 1/4, এবং তাদের মধ্যে কোণ হল π / 4 (অর্থাৎ, প্রথম লাইন সঙ্গতি-এক্সিস থেকে)।

আমরা খুঁজে বের করতে চাই যে দ্বিতীয় লাইনের m = ?

প্রথম ধাপ, আসুন উভয় লাইনের ঢালগুলির মধ্যে কোণ (θ) খুঁজে বের করা যাক:

tan(θ) = |m1 - m2| / (1 + m1 × m2)

প্রথম লাইনের m = 1/4, তাই:

tan(θ) = |1/4 - m2| / (1 + 1/4 × m2)

এখন, আমাদের θ = π / 4 (or 45°) ব্যাপার জানতে হবে, কিন্তু আমরা ইনভার্ট থেকে tan-1() এর সাথে θ = π / 4 খোদাই করি। যাইহোক, এটি নিশ্চিত না! এভাবে আমাদের থেকে সঠিক উপজাত খুঁজে পেতে, আমরা লক্ষ্য করি যে tan(π / 4) = 1

এবং আগের সমীকরণে m2 প্রতিস্থাপন, আমাদের আছে:

|1/4 - m2| / (1 + 1/4 × m2) = 1

এখন, যেহেতু 1/4 ইতিবাচক, লক্ষ্য করুন যে পরিণতি:

|1/4 - m2| = (1 + 1/4 × m2)

উভয় দিকে রাশি দিন, আমাদের আছে:

1/4 - m2 = (1 + 1/4 × m2)

পুনর্বিন্যাস করা:

-1/4m2 - m2 = 1

এখন, মূল অভিব্যক্তি থেকে গোষ্ঠী করে:

(-1/4 - 1) * m2 = 1

ফ্যাক্টর আউট সমগ্র (-5/4):

-5/4m2 = 1

এখন, বিপরীত ভাজক (বেছে নিয়ে) m2:

m2 = -4/5

অতএব, দ্বিতীয় লাইনের ঢাল হল -4/5।

চূড়ান্ত উত্তর হল:
{'Final Answer': 'm2 = -4/5'}

***********************A slope problem!

To find the point on the x-axis that is equidistant from points (9, 8) and (5, 6), we can use the concept of midpoint.

The distance between two points (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) is given by:

√((x2 - x1)^2 + (y2 - y1)^2)

Since we want to find a point on the x-axis that is equidistant from both points, its coordinates will be (x, 0).

The midpoint of two points (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) is given by:

((x1 + x2)/2, (y1 + y2)/2)

Equating the two expressions for distance, we get:

√((9 - x)^2 + 8^2) = √((5 - x)^2 + 6^2)

Squaring both sides and simplifying, we get:

(9 - x)^2 + 64 = (5 - x)^2 + 36

Expanding the squares and collecting like terms, we get:

x^2 - 18x + 81 + 64 = x^2 - 10x + 25 + 36

Simplifying further, we get:

-8x + 145 = -4x + 61

Subtracting 145 from both sides and adding 4x to both sides, we get:

-12x = -84

Dividing both sides by -12, we get:

x = 7

So, the point on the x-axis that is equidistant from points (9, 8) and (5, 6) is (7, 0).

The final answer is:
{'Final Answer' : 7}

***********************আমি সহায়তা করার জন্য এখানে।

প্রথমে, লাইনের মধ্য-বিন্দু খুঁজে পেতে, আমাদের সেগুলির x-কোঅর্ডিনেট এবং y-কোঅর্ডিনেটের যোগফলের সমষ্টি, তারপরে x-কোঅর্ডিনেট এবং y-কোঅর্ডিনেট দ্বারা 2:1 অনুপাতে ভাগ করতে হবে।

To determine if the points (4, 4), (3, 5), and (-1, -1) form a right-angled triangle with one of the sides being vertical, we can use the concept of slopes. Since it's a right-angled triangle, one pair of points will have a slope of 0, indicating that the line connecting them is horizontal.

First, let's calculate the slope between each pair of points:

1. Slope between (4, 4) and (3, 5):
   m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
   m = (5 - 4) / (3 - 4)
   m = 1 / (-1)
   m = -1

2. Slope between (4, 4) and (-1, -1):
   m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
   m = (-1 - 4) / (-1 - 4)
   m = (-5) / (-5)
   m = 1

3. Slope between (3, 5) and (-1, -1):
   m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
   m = (-1 - 5) / (-1 - 3)
   m = (-6) / (-4)
   m = 3/2

Since the slope between points (4, 4) and (-1, -1) is not 0, this line is not horizontal. However, to determine if these three points form a right-angled triangle with one side being vertical, we can check if the other two slopes satisfy the condition that their sum of squares equals 2.

We know the Pythagorean theorem states: a^2 + b^2 = c^2

Where 'c' is the hypotenuse. In this case, since we're looking for a right triangle with one side being vertical (which will be our height), let's say that our hypotenuse 'c' has length equal to the distance between (-1,-1) and (3,5). Our 'b', then would be the length of either horizontal leg. 

Let's calculate c:
The distance formula: c = sqrt((x2 - x1)^2 + (y2 - y1)^2)
c = sqrt((-1-3)^2 + (-1-5)^2) 
c = sqrt(16+36)
c = sqrt(52)

Now, let's say we use points (4, 4) and (3, 5). We'll call this one 'b'. First calculate the length:
b = sqrt((x2 - x1)^2 + (y2 - y1)^2)
b = sqrt((3-4)^2 + (5-4)^2) 
b = sqrt(1+1)
b = sqrt(2)

Now, our condition says that a^2+b^2=c^2. So, we plug in c and b:
a^2 + 2 = sqrt(52)^2

We have all the values for a,b,c. Plug them into the equation.
a^2+2=52
Subtract 2 from both sides of the equation.
a^2=50

Here's how to find the slope between each pair of points:

1. Slope between (-2, -1) and (4, 0):
   m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1)
   m = (0 - (-1))/(4 - (-2))
   m = 1/6

2. Slope between (4, 0) and (3, 3):
   m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1)
   m = (3 - 0)/(3 - 4)
   This slope is undefined as the denominator is zero.

   m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1)
   m = (2 - (-1))/(-3 - (-2))
   m = 3/-1
   m = -3

***********************A nice slope problem!

Let's break it down step by step:

**Step 1:** Find the slope of the line passing through points (5,1) and (6,0).

The slope of a line passing through two points (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) is given by:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

In this case, (x1, y1) = (5, 1) and (x2, y2) = (6, 0).

So, m = (0 - 1) / (6 - 5)
m = -1 / 1
m = -1

**Step 2:** Find the slope of the x-axis.

The equation of the x-axis is y = 0. The slope of this line is 0, since it's a horizontal line.

**Step 3:** Find the angle between the two lines.

We know that the slope of the first line is -1 and the slope of the second line (x-axis) is 0.

Let's use the formula:

tan(θ) = |m1| / |m2|

where θ is the angle between the two lines, m1 is the slope of the first line (-1), and m2 is the slope of the second line (0).

Plugging in the values, we get:

tan(θ) = |-1| / |0|
tan(θ) = 1 / 0

This is an indeterminate form! However, since the denominator is zero, we know that the tangent function will approach infinity or negative infinity as the angle approaches a certain value.

To find this value, let's recall that tan(90°) = ∞ (or -∞).

So, the angle between the two lines is:

θ = 90°

***********************১. y = x + 5
২. y = -x + 5

***********************A nice problem in slope of a line!

Let's break it down step by step.

We are given two points:

A = (-2, 0)
B = (0, 5)

Our goal is to find the equation of the line passing through these two points.

Here are the steps:

1. Find the slope of the line using the formula: m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where m is the slope and (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) are the coordinates of the two points.

Plugging in the values, we get:

m = (5 - 0) / (0 - (-2))
= 5 / 2
= 2.5

So, the slope of the line is 2.5.

Now that we have the slope, we can use it to find the equation of the line in point-slope form:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where (x1, y1) is one of the points on the line.

Let's choose point A = (-2, 0). Then the equation becomes:

y - 0 = 2.5(x - (-2))
y = 2.5(x + 2)

Simplifying, we get:

y = 2.5x + 5

So, the equation of the line is y = 2.5x + 5.

m = 1/4

where m is the slope of the line.

Now, let's use the point-slope form of a line to write its equation:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

We know that the inclination θ is given by tan(θ) = 1/4. This implies that for some fixed point (x1, y1), the slope m = 1/4.

To find this point (x1, y1), let's use the fact that the line passes through the origin O(0, 0). So, we can substitute x1 = 0 and y1 = 0 into the point-slope form:

y - 0 = m(x - 0)
y = mx

Now, since m = 1/4, we get:

y = (1/4)x

This is one equation of the lines for which tan(θ) = 1/4 holds.

To write the equation in the given format, let's use the slope-intercept form y = mx + c:

y - (-5/4)x = 0

Simplifying this gives us the final answer.

Let's break it down step by step.

We are looking for a line that passes through the points (-1, 0) and (4, 0). This means that we have two points on the x-axis, which makes things easier.

The general form of a linear equation is:

ax + by = c

Since the line passes through the point (-1, 0), we can substitute these values into the equation to get:

a(-1) + b(0) = c
- a = c

Similarly, since the line passes through the point (4, 0), we can substitute these values into the equation to get:

a(4) + b(0) = c
4a = c

Now we have two equations with one unknown variable, which is a. We can equate the two expressions for c to solve for a:

-a = 4a
-5a = 0
a = 0

So, the value of a is 0.

Now that we know a, we can substitute this value back into one of the equations to find b and c. Let's use the first equation:

-a = c
-0 = c
c = 0

So, b = 1 and c = 0.

The final answer is:

***********************A nice problem in slope of a line!

Let's break it down step by step.

We are given that Point A is (H, K) and the ratio 1:4 represents the division of a line. This means that for every 1 unit of length on one side of the point, there are 4 units of length on the other side.

To find the equation of the line that divides the points in this ratio, we need to use the concept of slope and intercepts.

Let's assume that Point A is (H, K) and Point B is (x, y). Since the ratio is 1:4, the distance between H and x is 4 times the distance between K and y.

Using the concept of similar triangles, we can set up a proportion:

(H - x) / (K - y) = 1/4

Simplifying this equation, we get:

H - x = 4(K - y)

Expanding and rearranging terms, we get:

x = H + 4y - 4K
y = K - 0.25(H - x)

Now, let's simplify the above equation to find a relationship between x and y.

Simplifying the second equation in the above step, we get:
x - 0.25H + 0.25y = K

Moving all terms to one side of the equation, we have:

x - 0.25y = 0.75K + 0.25H

Dividing both sides by -1, we get:

- x + 0.25y = -0.75K - 0.25H

Multiplying both sides by 4 to eliminate the fraction, we have:
-4x + y = -3K - H

Now, let's rewrite this equation in point-slope form, which is given as:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where (x1, y1) is a point on the line and m is the slope.

Comparing this with our previous step, we can see that:
x1 = 0.75K + 0.25H
y1 = K

Substituting these values into the point-slope form, we get:

y - K = (-3/4)(x - 0.75K - 0.25H)

Multiplying both sides by 4 to eliminate the fraction, we have:
4(y - K) = -3(x - 0.75K - 0.25H)

Expanding and simplifying this equation, we get:

4y - 4K = -3x + 2.25K + 0.75H

Now, let's rewrite this equation in the standard form (Ax + By = C), where A, B, and C are constants.

Grouping like terms on one side of the equation, we get:

-3x + 4y - 0.75H - 2.25K = 0

Finally, let's multiply both sides by -1 to make the coefficient of x positive.

This gives us the final answer in standard form:

3x - 4y + 0.75H + 2.25K = 0

So, the solution is:
{'Final Answer' : '3x - 4y + 0.75H + 2.25K = 0'}

**Assumptions**

* We assume that the linear relationship holds between the selling price and demand.
* The owner's goal is to maximize his sales volume while maintaining a certain profit margin.

**Mathematical Formulation**

Let x be the number of liters sold at 117/liter. We can set up an equation based on the owner's total revenue:

Revenue = (Number of liters sold at 114/liter × Price) + (Number of liters sold at 116/liter × Price)

Using the given values, we get:

 Revenue = (1080 × 114) + (1320 × 116)
 Revenue = 123,120 + 152,320
 Revenue = 275,440

Now, let's add x liters sold at 117/liter to the revenue equation:

Revenue = 275,440 + 117x

**Solving for x**

To find the value of x, we can equate the new revenue with the original revenue and solve for x:

275,440 + 117x = Revenue (at 114/liter and 116/liter)

Now, substitute the calculated revenue from above:

275,440 + 117x = 275,440

Subtracting 275,440 from both sides gives us:

117x = 0

Dividing by 117 (which is non-zero), we get:

x = 0 / 117
x = 0

## Step 1: Find the slope of the given line
To find the equation of the perpendicular line, we first need to determine the slope of the given line. Since the slope-intercept form of a linear equation is y = mx + c, where m is the slope, we can infer that the slope of the given line is 'm'.

## Step 2: Use point-slope form for the perpendicular line

***********************A nice problem in slope of a line!

To solve this, let's break it down step by step.

**Step 1: Find the equation of the line**

We have a point (0,2) and an angle of 2π/3 with the positive x-axis. We can use this information to find the slope of the line:

tan(2π/3) = m

where m is the slope of the line.

Using the unit circle or trigonometric identities, we can calculate:

tan(2π/3) = -√3

So, the slope of the line (m) is -√3.

Now that we have the slope, we can use the point-slope form of a linear equation to find the equation of the line:

y - 2 = m(x - 0)

Substituting m = -√3, we get:

y - 2 = -√3x

Simplifying and rearranging terms, we get:

y = -√3x + 2

This is the equation of the line.

**Step 2: Find the equation of the parallel line**

Since the two lines are parallel, they have the same slope. Therefore, the equation of the parallel line is also y = -√3x + k, where k is a constant.

To find k, we can use the fact that the two lines intersect at (0,2). Substituting x = 0 and y = 2 into the equation, we get:

2 = -√3(0) + k

Simplifying, we get:

k = 2

So, the equation of the parallel line is:

y = -√3x + 2

**Step 3: Find the point where the line intersects the y-axis**

To find the intersection point with the y-axis, we can set x = 0 in the equation:

y = -√3(0) + 2

Simplifying, we get:

y = 2

So, the line intersects the y-axis at (0,2).

**Step 4: Find the distance from the origin to the point where the line intersects the y-axis**

The distance between two points (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) is given by:

distance = √((x2 - x1)^2 + (y2 - y1)^2)

In this case, we have (0, 2) as the point where the line intersects the y-axis. The origin is (0, 0). Plugging in these values, we get:

distance = √((0 - 0)^2 + (2 - 0)^2)
= √(4)
= 2

So, the distance from the origin to the point where the line intersects the y-axis is 2 units.

**Step 5: Find the equation of the parallel line that intersects the y-axis at a distance of 2 units**

Since we already found the equation of the parallel line as y = -√3x + 2, we can simply use this equation.

The final answer is:

```json
{
  "Final Answer": "y = -√3x + 2"
}
```

***********************A nice problem in Forms of Equation of a line!

Let's denote the x-intercept and y-intercept of the line as (x, 0) and (0, y), respectively. Since their sum is 13, we can write:

x + y = 13

Now, let's use the point-slope form of a linear equation, which is:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where (x1, y1) is the given point (4, 4), and m is the slope.

Since we want to find the line passing through the point (4, 4) and intersecting with the intercepts at a distance of 13 units, we can assume that the slope is not zero. Let's denote the slope as m. Then, using the point-slope form, we get:

y - 4 = m(x - 4)

Now, let's find the intersection points by substituting x = 0 and y = 0 into the equation.

For x = 0:
y - 4 = m(0 - 4)
y - 4 = -4m
y = 4 - 4m

Similarly, for y = 0:
y - 4 = m(x - 4)
-4 = mx - 4m
mx + (4m - 4) = 0

Substituting the value of x from this equation into the first equation, we get:

y = -(4m - 4)/m

Now, substitute y = 4 - 4m and y = -(4m - 4)/m into the equation x + y = 13.

We have two equations with two variables. Let's solve for m!

Substitute y = 4 - 4m:
x + (4 - 4m) = 13
x - 4m + 4 = 13

Now, substitute y = -(4m - 4)/m:
x - ((4m - 4)/m) = 13
mx - (4m - 4) = 13m

Multiply the second equation by m to eliminate fractions:

xmx - (4m - 4) = 13m

We now have two equations with x and m. We can solve for x in terms of m using the first equation:

x = 17 - 4m + 4m
x = 17

Now substitute this value into one of the original equations to find m.

y = 4 - 4m
0 = 4 - 4m - 4
-8 = -4m
m = 2

With the value of m, we can now find y using either equation:

y = -(4m - 4)/m
y = -(4(2) - 4)/2
y = -4

Now that we have found x and y, which represent the intercepts of the line, we can write the final answer as a json object.

{'Final Answer' : [17, 4], [0,-4]}

ধারালো:

ঢাল (ভিত্তি-উচ্চতা অনুপাত) এমন সংখ্যা যা x-অর্ডিনেটকে y-অর্ডিনেট দ্বারা ভাগ করা হলে দুই বিন্দুর মধ্যে সংযোগকারী সেগমেন্টের চাপের অনুপাত।

যখন x এবং y-কোণগুলিকে ভাগ করা হয় তখন, ঢাল হল y-ইন্টারসেপ্ট / x-ইন্টারসেপ্ট।

এখানে, x-ইন্টারসেপ্ট 1, এবং y-ইন্টারসেপ্ট 0।

ঢাল = y-ইন্টারসেপ্ট / x-ইন্টারসেপ্ট
= 0/1
= 0

এর মানে হল যে (1, 0) এবং (2, 3) ভাগ করা লাইনটি অনুপাত 0:1-এর, যার মানে হল স্থির y = -x।

To find the equation of a line passing through two points and perpendicular to another line, we need to follow these steps:

1. Find the slope of the original line using the two given points.
2. Use the point-slope form of a line to write the equation of the new line that is perpendicular to the original line.

Let's go through each step:

**Step 1: Find the slope of the original line**

The slope (m) of a line passing through two points (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) can be found using the formula:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

Plugging in the values for the given points (4, 7) and (-5, 8), we get:

m = (8 - 7) / (-5 - 4)
= 1 / -9
= -1/9

So, the slope of the original line is -1/9.

**Step 2: Find the equation of the new line**

To find the equation of a line that is perpendicular to another line with slope m, we use the formula:

m' = -1/m (where m' is the slope of the new line)

In this case, since the original line has slope m = -1/9, the new line will have slope m' = -1 / (-1/9) = 9

Now that we have the slope of the new line (m' = 9), we can use the point-slope form of a line to write its equation. We'll use one of the given points, say (4, 7).

The point-slope form is:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

Plugging in the values for this point and the slope of the new line, we get:

y - 7 = 9(x - 4)
y - 7 = 9x - 36
y = 9x - 29

Therefore, the equation of the line passing through the points (4, 7) and (-5, 8), and perpendicular to the original line is:

**Final Answer:**

"Final Answer": "y = 9x - 29"

We know that the point (2, 4) lies on the first line, so we can substitute these coordinates into the equation y = mx + c to get:

4 = 2m + c

4 = -2m + c

2) 4 = -2m + c

m = 0

That means the second line is a horizontal line with an equation of y = c.

Now, let's substitute this value of m back into one of our original equations to find c. We'll use equation (1):

4 = 2(0) + c
c = 4

*************************Problem Solution**

We are given two points on a line, (H, 5) and (6, 3), and we know that this line intersects another line represented by the equation 7x - 9y - 19 = 0 at a right angle.

Our goal is to find the value of H.

**Step 1: Find the slope of the given line**

The slope-intercept form of a line is y = mx + c, where m is the slope and c is the y-intercept. To find the slope of the given line, we need to rewrite the equation in this form.

7x - 9y - 19 = 0
-9y = -7x + 19
y = (7/9)x - 19/9

So, the slope of the given line is m1 = 7/9.

**Step 2: Find the slope of the line perpendicular to the given line**

Since the lines intersect at a right angle, the slope of the second line (m2) will be the negative reciprocal of m1. Therefore,

m2 = -1/m1
= -9/7

**Step 3: Use the point-slope form of a line to find the equation of the perpendicular line**

Using the point-slope form, y - y1 = m(x - x1), where (x1, y1) is a point on the line. Let's use the point (H, 5).

y - 5 = (-9/7)(x - H)

**Step 4: Substitute this equation into the given equation to find the value of H**

We know that the two lines intersect at a right angle, so we can substitute the expression for y from the point-slope form into the given equation.

7x - 9(5 + (-9/7)(x - H)) - 19 = 0

Simplifying this equation will allow us to find the value of H.

**Step 5: Simplify and solve for H**

Expanding the equation:

7x - 45 - (81/7)x + 9H - 19 = 0
Combine like terms:
7x - (81/7)x + 9H - 64 = 0

Multiply both sides by 7 to eliminate the fraction:

49x - 81x + 63H - 448 = 0

Combine like terms:

-32x + 63H - 448 = 0
Add 448 to both sides:
-32x + 63H = 448

Divide by -32:
x - (63/32)H = -14
Multiply both sides by -1 to make the equation more manageable.
32x - 63H = 14
Now, we have an equation with x and H that we can use to solve for H.

**Step 6: Find the value of H using one of the points on the line**

We are given two points (H, 5) and (6, 3). Let's use the point (6, 3).

Substituting this point into the equation:

192 - 63H = 14

-63H = -178

Dividing by -63:

H = 178/63
= 2.7947 (rounded to four decimal places)

১ম সমীকরণের উভয় পাশে x এর অগ্রণী পদ, y এর অগ্রণী পদ এবং ডানদিকের মুলত উভয় দিকে 1 যোগ করা হয় (স্পষ্ট করা), এবং ধনাত্মক ও নেতিবাচক উভয় রৈখিক ফর্মে রূপান্তর দিয়া লেখা হয়। 

সুতরুপ, 3x + y = 1 

(উভয় বাহুর x-এর অগ্রণী পদের মূল) - শব্দটি (y-এর অগ্রণী পদ) = 3

-1/3 x + y = 1 

যেহেতু উভয় সমীকরণে y-এর আগে অগ্রণী পদ 1, তাই আমরা উভয় সমীকরণ থেকে x বিয়োগ করি।

-1/3x + y - y = 1 - 1 

(-1/3x) + (y-y) = 0 

এখানে, (-1/3x), (y-y) থেকে x বিয়োগ করা, আমরা -1/3x-1+0 = 0 পাই। স্পষ্ট করার জন্য 1 থেকে -1 অতিরিক্ত বিয়োগ করা, 

-4/3x = -1

(3/(-4)) x + y = -1

(উভয় দিক থেকে 1 অতিরিক্ত বিয়োগ করা) (স্পষ্ট করা), 

x = -3/4

এখন, আমাদের লাইনের y-অভিব্যক্তি খুঁজে বের করতে হবে। 

3x + y = 1

(4/3) (-3/4) + y = 1 

y = -3/4 * (3/4) + 1 
= -(9/16) + 1
= -(9/16) + (16/16)
= (16-9)/16
= 7/16

তাই, x এর মানে  -3/4, এবং y-এর মানে 7/16।

লাইনের ঢাল:

m = (y2-y1)/(x2-x1) = (7/16)-(0)/(-3/4)-0 

= (-7/16)/(-3/4)
= (-7 / -12)
= 7/12

এখানে, প্রথমে, আসুন দ্বিতীয় লাইনটির y-অভিব্যক্তি গণনা করি। 

প্রথমে, x + y = 1 

x এর জন্য 1 দিলে, আসুন y-অভিব্যক্তিটি গণনা করি। 

1 + y = 1

(2) থেকে (1), y-এর মানে: 
= 0

যেহেতু লাইনের ঢাল অবশ্যই অশূন্য হবে, আসুন y-এর জন্য 0 প্রতিস্থাপন করি: 

x + 0 = 1 

= x - 1 = 0 
= x = 1

অবশেষে, x-এর মান 1 হলে y-এর মান কী?

1 + y = 1

(1) থেকে (2), y-এর জন্য, 
y - y = 0
y - 0 = 0
y= 0 

লাইনের ঢাল:

m = (y2-y1)/(x2-x1) = (7/16)-(0)/(-3/4)-0 

= (-7/16)/(-3/4)
= (-7 / -12)
= 7/12

এখানে, আমরা x-এর জন্য 1 দিই (পূর্ববর্তী y-অভিব্যক্তি থেকে), 
(7/12) = (y2-y1)/(x2-x1)
= (-7/16)/(-3/4)

এখানে, 7/16 x 3/4 = -7/48 

যদিও উভয় সমীকরণ (y2-y1)/(x2-x1) = (-7/16)/(-3/4) এবং m = 7/12, 
(7/16)(-3/4) / ((3/4)x -1)) 

= (-7/16)*(4/3)

-7/48

লাইনের ঢাল: 

m = (y2-y1)/(x2-x1)
= 7/12

Now we have the slope (m = 1/7).

The perpendicular line will have a slope that is the negative reciprocal of this, which is -7.

y - 0 = -7(x - 5)
y = -7x + 35

Now we need to find the x-intercept of this line. We know it's a vertical line, so its equation will be in the form x = c.

***********************A nice linear algebra problem!

To find the slope of the line 5x - 6y + 4 = 0, we need to rewrite it in the slope-intercept form y = mx + c, where m is the slope.

First, let's rearrange the equation:

-6y = -5x - 4

Divide both sides by -6:

y = (5/6)x + 2/3

Now we can see that the slope of the line is 5/6.

The question asks for a parallel line to this one, which means it will have the same slope. However, since we are given a point (-4, 5) that lies on the original line and not on the parallel line, we cannot simply use the point-slope form with this point to find the equation of the parallel line.

Instead, let's recall that two lines are parallel if they have the same slope. So, the problem is equivalent to finding a line with slope 5/6 that passes through the point (-4, 5).

Using the point-slope form y - y1 = m(x - x1), where (x1, y1) is the given point and m is the slope:

y - 5 = (5/6)(x + 4)

Simplify the equation:

y - 5 = (5/6)x + 20/6

Combine like terms:

y - 5 = (5/6)x + 10/3

Add 5 to both sides:

y = (5/6)x + 10/3 + 5

To add 5 and 10/3, we need a common denominator, which is 3. So, convert the integer 5 to an equivalent fraction with a denominator of 3: 15/3.

Now we can add them:

y = (5/6)x + 25/3

= -(19 × 19)/(12 × 12)
= -361/144

Perpendicular distance between the two lines is given by formula:
d = |(-b0 - c0)/(√(a^2 + b^2))|

where a, b and c are coefficients of x, y and constant term in the equation of line ax + by + c = 0.

Here we have two lines: 
Line 1: 19x + 12y - 38 = 0
a1 = 19, b1 = 12, c1 = -38

Line 2: 38x + 24y + 70 = 0
a2 = 38, b2 = 24, c2 = 70

d1 = |(-12 - (-38))/(√(19^2 + 12^2))|
= |(26)/(√(361 + 144))|
= |26/(√505)|
≈ |26/22.5|

d2 = |(-24 - 70)/(√(38^2 + 24^2))|
= |(-94)/(√(1444 + 576))|

= √((14.87)^2 + (2.07)^2)
≈ √(221.37 + 4.29)
≈ √225.66
≈ 15

***********************A nice linear equation problem!

Let's solve it step by step:

**Step 1: Rearrange the equation to isolate y**

6x + 4y - 24 = 0

Subtract 6x from both sides:
4y - 24 = -6x

Add 24 to both sides:
4y = -6x + 24

Divide both sides by 4:
y = (-6x + 24) / 4

Simplify the fraction:
y = (-3x + 12) / 2

**Step 2: Write the equation in intercept form**

The intercept form of a line is Ax + By = C, where A and B are the x-intercept and y-intercept respectively. To convert our equation to intercept form, we need to rewrite it as:

3x - 6y + 24 = 0

Now, let's find the x-intercept (A) and y-intercept (B):

* x-intercept (A): Set y = 0 and solve for x:
3x - 6(0) + 24 = 0
3x + 24 = 0
3x = -24
x = -8

So, the x-intercept is A = -8.
* y-intercept (B): Set x = 0 and solve for y:
3(0) - 6y + 24 = 0
-6y + 24 = 0
-6y = -24
y = 4

So, the y-intercept is B = 4.

Let's break it down step by step:

**Step 1: Find the slope of the line**

The equation of the line is `4x - y = 0`, which can be rewritten as `y = 4x`. So, the slope of the line is `m = 4`.

**Step 2: Find the angle between the line and the positive x-axis**

Since the slope is `m = 4`, we know that the angle between the line and the positive x-axis is `tan^-1(4) ≈ 75.96°`. However, since this problem states that the line makes an angle of `135°` with the positive x-axis, I assume it's referring to the complement angle. So, the actual angle we're interested in is indeed `135°`.

**Step 3: Find the distance from point P to the line**

We can use the formula for the distance between a point and a line:

`d = |Ax + By + C| / √(A^2 + B^2)`

where `(x, y)` is point P (4, 1), and `Ax + By + C = 0` is the equation of the line.

Plugging in the values, we get:

`d = |4(4) - 1(1) + 0| / √(4^2 + (-1)^2)`
`d = |15| / √17`
`d ≈ 3.98`

The given lines are:

1) 9x + 6y - 7 = 0
2) 3x + 2y + 6 = 0

To find the equivalent line of (1), we can use the concept that two lines are parallel if their slopes are equal. Let's first convert both equations to slope-intercept form (y = mx + c).

For equation (1):

9x + 6y - 7 = 0
6y = -9x + 7
y = (-9/6)x + 7/6
y = (-3/2)x + 7/6

Now, let's convert equation (2) to slope-intercept form:

3x + 2y + 6 = 0
2y = -3x - 6
y = (-3/2)x - 3

Since the slopes of both lines are equal (-3/2), they are parallel. Now, let's find the equivalent line.

To find the equivalent line, we can use the fact that if (x1, y1) is a point on one line and (x2, y2) is a point on another line, then the equation of the line passing through the two points is given by:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where m is the slope.

Let's take the point (1, 0) which satisfies equation (1). Since we want to find an equivalent line that also passes through this point, we can use this point in the formula above.

Now, let's find the equivalent line. We'll use the fact that the slopes of both lines are equal (-3/2).

Let y = mx + c be the equation of the equivalent line. Since the slope is -3/2, we have m = -3/2.

We want to find the value of c. Let's substitute x = 1 and y = 0 into the formula above:

y - 0 = (-3/2)(x - 1)
-3 = (-3/2)(x - 1)

Solving for x, we get:

x = 5/3

Now that we have x = 5/3, let's substitute this value back into the equation y = mx + c. We'll use m = -3/2 and c is what we're looking for.

y = (-3/2)(5/3) + c
y = -5/2 + c

We want to find the value of c such that this line passes through the point (1, 0).

Substituting x = 1 and y = 0 into the equation above:

0 = (-5/2) + c
c = 5/2

Now we have found the values of m and c. Let's substitute these values back into the formula y = mx + c to get the equation of the equivalent line.

y = (-3/2)x + 5/2

To find the slope of a line passing through two points, we can use the formula:

Slope = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) are the coordinates of the two points.

In this case, the two points are (6, -4) and (-2, 8).

Plugging in these values into the formula, we get:

Slope = (8 - (-4)) / (-2 - 6)
= (8 + 4) / (-2 - 6)
= 12 / (-8)

Now, let's simplify this expression to find the final slope.

Since there is a negative sign in the denominator, we need to multiply both the numerator and denominator by -1 to make the denominator positive:

Slope = 12 / (-8) × (-1) / (-1)
= (12 × -1) / (8 × -1)
= -12 / -8
= 3/2

So, the slope of the line passing through points (6, -4) and (-2, 8) is 3/2.

Here's the solution as a JSON object:

{
"Final Answer": "3/2"
}

***********************A nice slope problem!

To find the slope of a line passing through two points, we can use the formula:

slope = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) are the coordinates of the two points.

In this case, the points are (7, 2) and (7, 8), so we can plug in the values:

slope = (8 - 2) / (7 - 7)
= 6 / 0
= undefined

Since the denominator is zero, this means that there is no slope to be found. This is because a vertical line has an undefined slope!

So, the solution is:
{'Final Answer' : 'Undefined'}

***********************আমি তোমার সাহায্য করতে চাইন.

এক্স-অক্ষের ইতিবাচক দিক দিয়ে 120 ° এর প্রবণতা তৈরি করে এমন লাইনের ope আলটি খুঁজে পেতে, আমরা y = mx + c সমীকরণ ব্যবহার করতে পারি, যেখানে m হল ঢাল।

যেহেতু ঢালটি 120 ° এর সমান, আমরা tan(120 °) = m সমীকরণটি ব্যবহার করতে পারি।

tan(120 °) = -√3 (tan 30 ° = 1/√3, এবং tan 60 ° = √3, এবং মনে রাখবেন যে tan 120 ° = tan (180 - 60) = - tan 60)

সুতরাং, y = -√3x + c

আমরা জানি যে লাইনটিকে x-অক্ষের ইতিবাচক দিক থেকে 120 ° এর প্রবণতা তৈরি করতে হবে, যার মানে x-অক্ষের ইতিবাচক দিক থেকে 120 ° এর প্রবণতা তৈরি করতে হবে। এটি x-অক্ষের নাকে যাওয়া 5 ইউনিটের দূরত্ব থাকতে পারে।

সুতরাং, আমরা -√3(5) + c = 0 (x-অক্ষের উপরে লাইনের ope আলটি)

c = √3 * 5

y = -√3x + √3 * 5

{

***********************A nice problem in slope of lines!

Let's break it down step by step.

Given:

* Two lines, let's call them line A and line B
* Angle between line A and line B is π/4 (or 45°)
* Slope of line A is 1/4

We need to find the slope of line B.

Recall that the angle between two lines is equal to the difference of their slopes, multiplied by -1. Mathematically:

tan(θ) = |m₁ - m₂|

where θ is the angle between the lines, and m₁ and m₂ are the slopes of the lines.

In this case, θ = π/4 (45°), and we know that tan(45°) = 1.

We also know that the slope of line A (m₁) is 1/4. So, let's plug in these values:

1 = |1/4 - m₂|

To find m₂, which is the slope of line B, we can solve for it:

|1/4 - m₂| = 1

Since the angle between the lines is positive (45°), we know that the slopes are on opposite sides of the vertical line. Therefore, the expression |...| can be rewritten as -(1/4 - m₂) or 1/4 - m₂.

Let's assume the latter:

1/4 - m₂ = 1

Solving for m₂:

m₂ = -3/4

So, the slope of line B is -3/4.

Therefore, the final answer is:

{'Final Answer' : '-3/4'}

***********************A nice linear algebra problem!

Let's break it down step by step:

1. We are given two points on a line, (0, 8) and (6, 10). The equation of this line can be found using the point-slope form:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where m is the slope of the line.

First, let's find the slope:
m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (10 - 8) / (6 - 0)
= 2/6
= 1/3

Now that we have the slope, we can write the equation of the line in point-slope form:

y - 8 = (1/3)(x - 0)

To get it in slope-intercept form (y = mx + b), let's simplify:
y - 8 = (1/3)x
y = (1/3)x + 8

2. Now, we are given another point on a line, (10, 14) and (x, 26). We know that the first line is L1 (y = x/3 + 8), and this new line is perpendicular to L1.

The slope of L1 is 1/3, so the slope of the perpendicular line (L2) will be -3.

We can write the equation of L2 in point-slope form:
y - y1 = m(x - x1)
26 - 14 = (-3)(x - 10)

Simplifying this expression gives us:
12 = (-3)x + 30
(-3)x = -18
x = 6

So, the value of x is 6.

Here's the solution in JSON format:

{
"Final Answer": "6"
}

***********************আমি তোমার সহায়তা করবেন।

প্রথমে, আমাদের চতুর্ভুজের শীর্ষগুলির স্থানাঙ্কগুলি বিবেচনা করতে হবে:

(-6, 7), (2, 9), (7, -3), এবং (-2, 0)

আমাদের স্পর্শক-অঞ্চল খুঁজে বের করতে হবে।

ধরা যাক (x_1, y_1) = (-6, 7), (x_2, y_2) = (2, 9), (x_3, y_3) = (7, -3), এবং (x_4, y_4) = (-2, 0)

স্পর্শক-অঞ্চল খুঁজে বের করতে, আমরা নিম্নলিখিত ধাপগুলি অনুসরণ করতে পারি:

1. যেকোনো দুই শীর্ষের মধ্যে একটি রেখা আঁকুন।
2. অন্য সব শীর্ষের জন্য, প্রথম দুই শীর্ষের মধ্যে খোলা চাপের উঠান-কেন্দ্রের স্থানাঙ্ক গণনা করুন।
3. এই চাপের উঠান-কেন্দ্রের স্থানাঙ্কটি, যা (x_c, y_c), হবে স্পর্শক-অঞ্চলের কেন্দ্র।

এখানে, আমরা (-6, 7) এবং (2, 9) এর মধ্যে একটি রেখা আঁকতে পারি। অন্যান্য শীর্ষগুলির জন্য, আমরা চাপের উঠান-কেন্দ্রের স্থানাঙ্কটি গণনা করতে নিম্নলিখিত সূত্রগুলি ব্যবহার করতে পারি:

x_c = ((x_1 + x_2) / 2, (y_1 + y_2) / 2)

x_c = ((-6 + 2) / 2, (7 + 9) / 2)
= (-2, 8)

এখন যেকোনো দুই শীর্ষের মধ্যে রেখা আঁকুন।

(-6, 7) ও (2, 9)

y = 7 + ((9 - 7) / (2 - (-6))) * (x + 6)
= 7 + (2/8) * x
= 7 + (1/4)x

এক্সপ্রেশনের গুণিতকটি, y = 7 + (1/4)x, হল যে দুই শীর্ষের মধ্যে রেখার সমীকরণ।

এখন, অন্য সব শীর্ষের জন্য চাপের উঠান-কেন্দ্রের স্থানাঙ্ক গণনা করতে, আমরা এই ধাপটি পুনরাবৃত্তি করতে পারি।

(-6, 7) ও (2, 9)

x_c = ((-6 + 2) / 2, (7 + 9) / 2)
= (-2, 8)

(2, 9) ও (7, -3)

y = 9 - (3/5)(x - 2)
= 9 - (3/5)x + (6/5)

এক্সপ্রেশনের গুণিতকটি, y = 9 - (3/5)x + (6/5), হল যে দুই শীর্ষের মধ্যে রেখার সমীকরণ।

(-2, 0) ও (7, -3)

y = (-3/5)x + 6/5

এক্সপ্রেশনের গুণিতকটি, y = (-3/5)x + 6/5, হল যে দুই শীর্ষের মধ্যে রেখার সমীকরণ।

এখন আমরা চাপের উঠান-কেন্দ্রের স্থানাঙ্ক গণনা করি:

***********************I'd be happy to help you find the slope of the line that passes through the mid-point and is divided by the line segment joining points P(2, -2) and B(10, 2).

First, let's calculate the coordinates of the mid-point. We can use the midpoint formula:

Midpoint = ((x1 + x2)/2, (y1 + y2)/2)

Plugging in the values for points P and B, we get:

Midpoint = ((2 + 10)/2, (-2 + 2)/2) = (6, 0)

Now that we have the coordinates of the mid-point, let's calculate the slope of the line segment joining points P and B. We can use the slope formula:

Slope = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1)

Plugging in the values for points P and B, we get:

Slope = (2 - (-2))/(10 - 2) = 4/8 = 1/2

The line that passes through the mid-point and is divided by the line segment joining points P and B will have a slope equal to half of this value. Therefore, the slope of the desired line is:

Slope = (1/2)/2 = 1/4

So, the final answer is:

{'Final Answer' : '1/4'}

***********************A nice trigonometry problem!

To find the slope of a line that makes an angle of 60° with the positive y-axis, we can use the fact that the slope is equal to the tangent of the angle.

Let's denote the slope as m. We know that:

m = tan(60°)

Using the properties of the tangent function, we can rewrite this as:

m = √3

So, the final answer is:

{'Final Answer': 3**0.5}

***********************I'd be happy to help you solve this problem.

To determine if four points are collinear, we can calculate the slopes of all possible pairs of lines formed by these points and check if any two lines have the same slope. If they do, then the points are collinear.

Let's find the slopes:

1. Between (-2,-1) and (4,0):

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (0 - (-1)) / (4 - (-2))
= 1 / 6

2. Between (4,0) and (3,3):

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (3 - 0) / (3 - 4)
= -3

3. Between (3,3) and (-3,2):

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)
= (2 - 3) / (-3 - 3)
= -1/6

We can see that the slope of the line between points 1 and 2 is 1/6, which is not equal to any other slope. This means these four points are not collinear.

***********************I'd be happy to help you find the slope of the line joining the points (5,1) and (6,0).

The formula for finding the slope of a line joining two points is:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where m is the slope and (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) are the coordinates of the two points.

Plugging in the values, we get:

m = (0 - 1) / (6 - 5)
= -1/1
= -1

So, the slope of the line joining the points (5,1) and (6,0) is -1.
