***********************১. ope আলটি অন্য লাইনের ope আলের দ্বিগুণ
২. ope আল এবং তার স্পর্শকের মধ্যে 1/3 এর মধ্যে।

সমাধান:
ধরা যাক ope আলের দৈর্ঘ্য x। তারপর, অন্য লাইনের ope আলের দৈর্ঘ্য 2x।

ধরা যাক স্পর্শকের মধ্য দিয়ে অতিক্রম করা ope আলের দৈর্ঘ্য y।

স্পর্শকের মধ্য দিয়ে ope আল এবং অন্য লাইনের ope আলের মধ্যে 1/3 এর মধ্যে রয়েছে, তাই:

y = (1/3) \* x

স্পর্শকের মধ্য দিয়ে ope আল এবং অন্য লাইনের ope আলের মধ্যে ভাগ হওয়া কোণটি 1/3 রাখিতে, স্পর্শক অন্য লাইনের ope আলের 2x-এর মধ্যে y ভাগ করা 1:1 হবে।

তাই, (2x - y) / y = 1/3

পুনর্বিন্যাস, আমরা পাই:

2x - y = (1/3)y

y-কে সরান, আমরা পাই:

2x = (4/3)y

y-এ x থেকে বিয়োগ করে, আমরা পাই:

x = (1/3)y

এখন, y এর জন্য আমাদের একটি অভিব্যক্তি রয়েছে। আসুন x থেকে y পৃথক করা যাক:

y = 3x

এখন, ope আলের দৈর্ঘ্য খুঁজে বের করার জন্য y-তে x প্রতিস্থাপন করা যাক:

x = 3x

x এর মান খুঁজে বের করতে, আমরা উভয় দিকে 3 দ্বারা ভাগ করি:

x = 6

সুতরাং, ope আলের দৈর্ঘ্য 6।

চূড়ান্ত উত্তর:
{'Final Answer' : '6'}

***********************৫. y - 5 = 0

***********************The problem asks for finding the equation of a line passing through point (0, 5) and parallel to the line y = -2x.

To find the equation of a line that is parallel to another line, we can use the fact that parallel lines have the same slope. The slope of the given line y = -2x is -2.

Since the line we're looking for passes through point (0, 5), its slope will also be -2. Using the point-slope form of a linear equation, which is y - y1 = m(x - x1), where m is the slope and (x1, y1) is a point on the line, we can write the equation as:

y - 5 = -2(x - 0)

Simplifying this equation gives us:

y - 5 = -2x

Adding 5 to both sides of the equation results in:

y = -2x + 5

Therefore, the equation of the line is y = -2x + 5.

Here's the solution as a JSON object:

```json
{
  "Final Answer": "-2x + 5"
}
```

***********************A nice problem in Forms of Equation of a line!

Let's use the two points (3, 1) and (5, 7) to find the equation of the line that passes through them.

We can use the point-slope form of a linear equation, which is:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where (x1, y1) is one of the points, and m is the slope of the line.

Let's choose the point (3, 1) as (x1, y1). Then, we can plug in the values as follows:

y - 1 = m(x - 3)

Now, let's find the slope (m) using the two points. The slope is given by:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) are the two points.

Plugging in the values, we get:

m = (7 - 1) / (5 - 3)
= 6 / 2
= 3

So, the slope of the line is 3.

Now that we have the slope, we can plug it back into the point-slope form equation to get the final answer:

y - 1 = 3(x - 3)

To make it more standard, let's rewrite the equation in the form y = mx + b, where m is the slope and b is the y-intercept.

Expanding the equation, we get:

y - 1 = 3x - 9

Adding 1 to both sides of the equation, we get:

y = 3x - 8

So, the final answer is:

{
  "Final Answer": "y = 3x - 8"
}

***********************১. যে রেখাগুলির জন্য ট্যান θ = 1/4 এর সমীকরণটি লিখুন
ইউনিভার্সাল ফর্ম:

y - y0 = (tan(θ))/(1 + tan^2(θ)) * (x - x0)

যেখানে (x0, y0) হল প্রাথমিক বিন্দু।

আসুন θ = 45° ধরি। এটি tan(θ) = 1/√2 ফলাফলের জন্য একটি মান-ভিত্তিক অনুপাত দেবে।

tan^2(45°) = (1/√2)^2 = 1/2

tan(θ)/(1 + tan^2(45)) = (1/√2)/((1 + 1/2))

= (1/√2)/((3/2))

= √2/3 বিভাজনের আগে, উত্তরটি হোল্ড

এখন, সমীকরণটি y - y0 = (√2/3) * (x - x0)

যাইহোক, আমরা প্রাথমিক বিন্দুর স্থানাঙ্ক জানি না। এখন, যে লাইনের জন্য y - y0 = (√2/3) * (x - x0) ট্যান θ = 1/4 সমীকরণটি লিখুন

tan^2(θ) = tan^2(45°) = 1
y - y0 = (√2/3) * (x - x0)

y - y0 = (√2/3)/((1 + √2/3))

যাইহোক, আমরা প্রাথমিক বিন্দুর স্থানাঙ্ক জানি না। y0 = -5/4

y - (-5/4) = (√2/3)/((1 + √2/3)) * (x - 0)

যে লাইনের জন্য y - (-5/4) = (√2/3)/((1 + √2/3)) * (x - 0), এটি

y + 5/4 = (√2/3)/((1 + √2/3)) * x

সুতরাং, যে লাইনের জন্য y + 5/4 = (√2/3)/((1 + √2/3)) * x ট্যান θ = 1/4

এক্স-ইন্টারসেপ্টের মোড হ'ল:

x = ((y + 5/4) * (1 + √2/3))/ (√2/3)

x = (3( y + 5/4 )+√6)/ 2

y-ইন্টারসেপ্ট হল:

y = ((x*(1 + √2/3)) - (√2/3))/((√2/3)+1)

২. Y-intercept:
y = (-5/4) - ( (√2/3)/(( 1+√2/3))) * 0

যাইহোক, আমরা y-এর স্থির বিন্দুর জন্য লাইনের Y-intercept খুঁজে পেতে চাই। 

y = -5/4

Y-intercept-এর মোড:

y = - 5/4

***********************আমি এই সমস্যার সমাধান করার জন্য তৈরি।

ট্যান θ = 1/6 দেওয়া আছে। এখানে, আমরা θ-এর সমাধান করে লাইনের ঢাল পাব।

tan(θ) = 1/6

যেহেতু tan(θ) = 1/6, আমরা θ-এর জন্য পিথাগোরিয়ান সূত্র ব্যবহার করে এটি সমাধান করতে পারি:

tan(θ) = opposite side / adjacent side

যেহেতু tan(θ) = 1/6, আমরা উভয় দিকের সংখ্যাগুলি 6 এবং 1 হতে দিই:

6x + y = c

যেহেতু x-intercept-টি 6, আমরা y = 0 সেট করে প্রথমে x-intercept-এর জন্য সমাধান করব:

6x + 0 = c
6x = c
x = c/6

আমাদের y-intercept-টিতে -1 রয়েছে, তাই আমরা x = 0 সেট করি:

0 + y = -1
y = -1

এখন, আমরা লাইনের ঢাল-এর জন্য x-ইন্টারসেপ্ট এবং y-ইন্টারসেপ্ট-কে ব্যবহার করতে পারি:

ঢাল (m) = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1)

আমাদের y1 = 0, x1 = 6, y2 = -1, এবং x2 = 0 আছে। তাই,

m = (-1-0) / (0-6)
m = -1/6

সুতরাং, লাইনের ঢাল হল -1/6।

{'Final Answer' : '-1/6'}

***********************A nice problem in slope of a line!

Let's break it down step by step:

We know that the line passes through two points, (-1, y1) and (4, y2).

Since the line passes through these two points, we can use the slope formula to find the slope of the line:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

where m is the slope, and (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) are the coordinates of the two points.

Plugging in the values, we get:

m = (y2 - (-1)) / (4 - (-1))
= (y2 + 1) / 5

Now, we want to find the equation of the line in slope-intercept form, which is y = mx + b, where m is the slope and b is the y-intercept.

We know that the y-intercept is -1, so we can write:

y = (y2 + 1) / 5 x - 1

Simplifying this expression, we get:

y = (y2 + 1)x/5 - 1
= ((y2/5 + 1/5)x - 1)

Now, to find the value of y2, let's plug in the coordinates (-1, y1) and (4, y2) into this equation. 

We get: 
y1 = ((y2)/5+1/5)*(-1)-1
and 
y2=((y2)/5+1/5)*4-1

After solving these two equations, we get the values of y1 and y2.

Finally, plugging in the value of y2 into the equation for the line, we get:

y = (y2 + 1)x/5 - 1

Simplifying this expression, we get:

y = ((32)/5+1/5)x-1
= (33/5)x-1


So, the final answer is:
{'Final Answer': '6.6x-1'}

***********************আমি তোমার সমস্যাটি সমাধান করতে চাই!

ধরুন, A = (3, 0), B = (-2, -2), এবং C = (8, 2)

পয়েন্ট A থেকে B পর্যন্ত ভেক্টর:
BA = (Bx - Ax, By - Ay) = (-2 - 3, -2 - 0) = (-5, -2)

এবং, পয়েন্ট A থেক্ত C পর্যন্ত ভেক্টর:
AC = (Cx - Ax, Cy - Ay) = (8 - 3, 2 - 0) = (5, 2)

এখন, BA এবং AC-এর সাধারণ ভেক্টর গণনা করি:

BA × AC = (-5, -2) × (5, 2) = -10 + 4 = -6

লাইনের সমীকরণ x-coordinates-এর জন্য বিভিন্ন ধরণের y-intercept:
y - y1 = m(x - x1)

প্রথম, আমাদের BA থেকে C পর্যন্ত লাইনের সমীকরণ খুঁজে বের করতে হবে:
y - (-2) = (1/6)(x - 3)

এটি x-coordinates-এর y-intercept, y0 = -2, দিয়ে লাইনের সমীকরণ।

এখন, তিনটি বিন্দু (3, 0), (-2, -2), এবং (8, 2) পরীক্ষা করা যাক:

1. P = (3, 0)
y - y0 = m(x - x0)
0 - (-2) = (1/6)(3 - 3)
সত্য

2. Q = (-2, -2)
-2 + 2 = (1/6)(8 - 3)

-4 = (5/6)
না

3. R = (8, 2)
2 - (-2) = (1/6)(8 - 3)
4 = (5/6)
না

***********************I'd be happy to help you solve this problem.

To find the equation of the line that passes through point (H, K) and divides the axis of ratio 1:4 equally, we need to follow these steps:

## Step 1: Find the coordinates of the two points on the axis
Since the axis has a ratio of 1:4, let's assume the x-coordinate of the first point is H and the y-coordinate of the second point is K. The other two points will be (H + 4*H, K) = (5H, K) and (H/4, K).

## Step 2: Find the slope of the line
To find the equation of the line that passes through these two points, we need to calculate its slope. Since we know the coordinates of the points, we can use the formula for slope:

m = (y2 - y1)/(x2 - x1)

where (x1, y1) and (x2, y2) are the coordinates of the two points.

## Step 3: Plug in the values
Let's plug in the values of the two points we found earlier into the formula:

m = ((K - K)/4H) = 0

The slope is zero!

## Step 4: Use the point-slope form to write the equation of the line
Since the slope is zero, the line is horizontal. Therefore, the y-coordinate of any point on the line is equal to K.

y = K

This is the final answer.

The final answer is: $\boxed{0}$

***********************A nice problem in forms of equations of a line!

Let's break it down step by step.

We are given that a line passing through point (2, 13) is perpendicular to another line. This means the slopes of these two lines are negative reciprocals of each other.

Let the slope of the first line be m1. Since it passes through (2, 13), its equation can be written as:

y - 13 = m1(x - 2)

We need to find the slope m1 of this line.

Since the second line is perpendicular to the first one, its slope will be the negative reciprocal of m1, let's call it m2. The equation of the second line can then be written as:

y - y0 = m2(x - x0)

where (x0, y0) is a point on this line.

Since the two lines are perpendicular, their slopes satisfy:

m1 × m2 = -1

We also know that the two lines intersect at the point (2, 13), which means they have a common solution. We can substitute x = 2 and y = 13 into both equations to find their intersection points.

Equating the first equation with y = 13:

13 - 13 = m1(2 - 2)

This simplifies to 0 = 0, which doesn't give us any information about m1. We need to use another point on the line to determine its slope.

Let's assume a point (x0, y0) is given that lies on both lines. Then:

m1(x0 - 2) + 13 = y0 ... (1)

Substitute y0 from the second equation into (1):

m1(x0 - 2) + 13 = m2(x0 - x0) + y0

Since the two lines intersect at point (x0, y0), we can set m1(x0 - 2) equal to y0 - 13:

y0 - 13 = m2(2 - x0)

Substitute this expression for m2 into the equation of the second line:

y - y0 = -(m1(x0 - 2) + 13)(x - x0)

Simplify and rearrange terms to obtain the final form of the first line's equation.

***********************আমি তোমার সমস্যা সমাধানের জন্য সহায়ক।

1. 2π/3 এর কোণ তৈরি করে বিন্দু (0,2) এর মাধ্যমে লাইনের সমীকরণ খুঁজে পেতে, আমাদের কোণ 2π/3-এর ট্যানজেন্ট অবস্থিত হবে।
2. ট্যানজেন্টের স্থানাঙ্ক (a, 0) খুঁজে পেতে, আমরা অনুভূমিক দূরত্ব হিসাবে x = |a| এবং যেহেতু রেখাটি 2π/3 কোণ তৈরি করে, আমাদের y-অক্ষের সাথে কোণ 2π/3 আছে। এটি y = a*tan(2π/3) হিসাবে প্রকাশ করা যেতে পারে।
3. সমীকরণ x^2 + (y - 2)^2 = a^2 ব্যবহার করে, আমরা y-অক্ষের থেকে লাইনের দূরত্ব খুঁজে পেতে পারি। a^2 = x^2 + (y - 2)^2
4. y-অক্ষের সাথে 2π/3 কোণ এবং |a| = 2 দিয়ে, y = a*tan(2π/3) হলে a^2 = 4 + (y - 2)^2।
5. x-অক্ষের সাথে y = a*tan(2π/3) এবং y = x * tan(2π/3) তৈরি করে, আমরা দেখতে পাই যে 0 = (x - a)*tan(2π/3), x = a।

6. এখন, y-অক্ষের সাথে 2π/3 কোণ দিয়ে, y = x * tan(2π/3)।
7. x - বিন্দু (0,2)-এর মধ্য দিয়ে যাওয়া 2π/3-কোণ তৈরি করা y = a*tan(2π/3) হল x = 0, 0 = (x - a)*tan(2π/3), 0 = (x - a)*√3
8. যেহেতু আমাদের বিন্দু (0,2) এর মধ্য দিয়ে y-অক্ষের সাথে 2π/3 কোণ তৈরি করা হয়েছে, x = 0 লাইনের এটি দেখায়।
9. সম্পর্ক a^2 = 4 + (y - 2)^2 ব্যবহার করে, x = a আছে y-অক্ষের থেকে দূরত্ব।
10. এই মানগুলি প্রয়োগ করে, a = 2।
11. অনুভূমিক x-অক্ষের সাথে y = x * tan(2π/3) এবং y = 0x - (2/tan(2π/3)) x = a।
12. যেহেতু x = a, a = 2, y-অক্ষের সাথে দূরত্ব খুঁজে পেতে এই মানগুলি প্রয়োগ করা, a^2 = 4 + (y - 2)^2।
13. y-অক্ষের সাথে দূরত্ব হিসাবে x = a, x = 0।

14. এর পরে, আমাদের লাইনের সমীকরণ খুঁজে বের করা উচিত।

এখন যদি আমরা সহজ করি, তারপর আমরা উভয় থেকে 4 বিয়োগ করব। এটি দেয়: 

151. x^2 - y^2 = 0.

আমরা উভয় পাশে (x+y)(xy) ফ্যাক্টর করি, এবং যেহেতু xy=4-0=4 সমীকরণ x^2-y^2=0

152. (x+y)(xy)=0 

153. xy=4

উভয় পাশা থেকে 4 বিয়োগ করে, আমরা সহজে লিখতে পারি: 

চূড়ান্ত উত্তর হল xy=4।

***********************A nice geometry problem!

Let's break it down step by step:

**Given information**

* The point is (4, 4)
* The line intersects the axes at two points whose sum of coordinates is 13
* We need to find the equation of this line

**Step 1: Find the intercepts**

Let's say the x-intercept is (a, 0) and the y-intercept is (0, b). Since their sum of coordinates is 13, we can write:

a + 0 = 13
0 + b = 13

From these equations, we get a = 13 and b = 13.

**Step 2: Find the equation of the line**

The general form of a line with intercepts (a, 0) and (0, b) is:

x/a + y/b = 1

Substituting a = 13 and b = 13 into this equation, we get:

x/13 + y/13 = 1
=> x + y = 13

**Step 3: Check if the point lies on the line**

We can substitute the coordinates of the point (4, 4) into the equation to check if it satisfies the equation.

4 + 4 = 8 ≠ 13

Oops! It looks like we made a mistake. The correct sum should be 13, not 8.

**Revisiting Step 2**

Let's go back to finding the intercepts. We know that the x-intercept is (a, 0) and the y-intercept is (0, b). Since their product is also equal to a * b = c^2, where c is some number, we can write:

a * b = 13^2
=> ab = 169

**Finding a and b**

We need to find two numbers whose product is 169. These numbers are 1 and 169 (since 1*169=169).

So, the intercepts are (1, 0) and (0, 169). However, we want their sum of coordinates to be 13. Therefore, the correct intercepts should be a = 7 and b = 6.

**Step 2: Find the equation of the line**

Using the correct intercepts, the equation of the line is:

x/7 + y/6 = 1
=> 6x + 7y = 42

Now we can check if the point lies on this line:

4 + (7*4) = 34 ≠ 42

Oops! It looks like the point does not satisfy the equation.

**Step 3: Find a parallel line**

We know that the original point was (4, 4). A parallel line to x/7 + y/6 = 1 will have the same slope, so its equation is of the form:

x/7 + y/k = 1

where k is some number.

Since we want this line to pass through (4, 4), we can substitute these coordinates into the equation and solve for k:

4/7 + 4/k = 1
=> 4/k = 3/7
=> k = 28/3

The correct equation of the parallel line is therefore:

x/7 + y/(28/3) = 1
=> x/7 + (3/28)y = 1

Multiplying both sides by 28, we get:

4x + (3/7)y = 28

**Step 4: Check if the point lies on this line**

Finally, let's check if the original point (4, 4) satisfies this equation:

4(4) + (3/7)(4) = 16 + 12/7 = 16 + 1.71 (approximately)
= 17.71 ≠ 28

Oops! The point still doesn't satisfy the equation.

**The correct answer**

Wait a minute... We've been going around in circles for too long!

Let's simplify things. Instead of finding the intercepts, we can directly use the point-slope form of a line:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where (x1, y1) is the given point and m is the slope.

The slope of the line passing through the given point and the intersection with the axes will be zero since it's parallel to the x-axis. So, we can write:

y - 4 = 0(x - 4)
=> y = 4

This means that the y-intercept is indeed (0, 4). The sum of its coordinates is 4.

Since the problem states that the intercepts have a sum of 13 and one of them is (0, 4), the other intercept must be (13 - 4, 0) = (9, 0).

**The final equation**

The line passes through the point (4, 4) and has an x-intercept at (9, 0). Since it's a straight line, we can use the two-point form:

(x1y2 - y1x2)/(y2 - y1) = x
=> (4*0 - 4*9)/(0 - 4) = x
=> -36/(-4) = x
=> 9 = x

This means that the line passes through the point (9, 0).

Using this point and the given point (4, 4), we can write:

y - 4 = m(x - 4)

Since the slope is zero, we have:

y - 4 = 0
=> y = 4

This means that the line has a constant y-value of 4.

However, since the problem states that the intercepts have a sum of 13 and one of them is (0, 4), the other intercept must be (9, 0).

So, we can write:

x/9 + y/4 = 1

This is the correct equation of the line.

**The final answer**

Here's the final answer in JSON format:

{
"Final Answer": "x/9 + y/4 = 1"
}

3. Substituting the values given in the problem statement into this formula:

***********************I'd be happy to help you find the equation of the line passing through point (-5, 7) and perpendicular to the line segment connecting points (4, 7) and (-5, 8).

To solve this problem, I'll follow these steps:

1. Find the slope of the line segment connecting points (4, 7) and (-5, 8).
2. Since we want a line perpendicular to this line segment, find its negative reciprocal.
3. Use point-slope form with the given point (-5, 7) and the slope found in step 2.

Let's start:

**Step 1: Find the slope of the line segment connecting points (4, 7) and (-5, 8).**

The slope \(m\) between two points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_2, y_2)\) is given by:
\[ m = \frac{y_2 - y_1}{x_2 - x_1} \]

Here, \((x_1, y_1) = (4, 7)\) and \((x_2, y_2) = (-5, 8)\).

Plugging in the values:
\[ m = \frac{8 - 7}{-5 - 4} = \frac{1}{-9} = -\frac{1}{9} \]

So, the slope of the line segment connecting points (4, 7) and (-5, 8) is \(-\frac{1}{9}\).

**Step 2: Find the negative reciprocal of the slope.**

The negative reciprocal of \(m\) is:
\[ m' = -\frac{1}{m} \]

Given that \(m = -\frac{1}{9}\), we find its negative reciprocal as follows:
\[ m' = -\frac{1}{-\frac{1}{9}} = 9 \]

**Step 3: Use point-slope form with the given point and the slope.**

The point-slope form of a line is given by:
\[ y - y_1 = m(x - x_1) \]

With the given point \(x_1, y_1 = (-5, 7)\) and the slope \(m = 9\), we substitute these values into the formula:
\[ y - 7 = 9(x + 5) \]

Simplifying this equation gives us the equation of the line passing through the point and perpendicular to the line segment connecting points (4, 7) and (-5, 8).

The final answer is: 
{'Final Answer' : 'y - 7 = 9(x + 5)'

Let's break it down step by step:

We have two lines:

Line 1: 3x - 4y + 12 = 0
Line 2: 3x - 4y + 7 = 0

To find the distance between these two parallel lines, we can use the formula:

where (A, B) are the coefficients of x and y in the line equations.

In this case, A = 3 and B = -4.

First, let's find the value of D, which is the difference between the constants on the right-hand side of the two line equations:

D = 12 - 7
= 5

Now we can plug in the values into the formula:

Distance = |5| / √(3² + (-4)²)
= 5 / √(9 + 16)
= 5 / √25
= 5 / 5
= 1

***********************A nice problem in slope of a line!

Let's break it down step by step.

We are given that the line passes through the point (2, 4) and is perpendicular to the line y = mx + c. This means that the slope of the line we are looking for is the negative reciprocal of m.

So, let's find the equation of the line passing through (2, 4) with an unknown slope m:

y - 4 = m(x - 2)

Now, since this line is perpendicular to y = mx + c, the product of their slopes is -1. Therefore:

m × m = -1

Solving for m, we get:

m² = -1
m = ±√(-1)
m = ±i (where i is the imaginary unit)

But wait! We are dealing with real numbers here. So, let's re-evaluate our approach.

The line y = mx + c has a slope of m and a y-intercept of c. Since it's perpendicular to the line passing through (2, 4), we can use the fact that the product of their slopes is -1:

m × (-m) = -1

Simplifying, we get:

-m² = -1
m² = 1
m = ±1

Now, we have two possible values for m: +1 and -1.

Let's find the equation of the line passing through (2, 4) with a slope of +1:

y - 4 = 1(x - 2)
y - 4 = x - 2
y = x - 2 + 4
y = x + 2

Comparing this with y = mx + c, we see that m = 1 and c = 2.

So, the solution is:

{'Final Answer': 'm=1,c=2'}

Distance = |Ax + By + C| / √(A^2 + B^2)

where (x, y) is the point, and Ax + By + C = 0 is the equation of the line.

In this case, A = 3, B = -4, C = -16, x = 1, and y = 5. Plugging these values into the formula:

Distance = |(3)(1) - (4)(5) - 16| / √((3)^2 + (-4)^2)
= |3 - 20 - 16| / √(9 + 16)
= |-33| / √25
= 33 / 5

***********************A nice problem in slope of a line!

To find the equation of the right bisector of the line joining two points (5, 6) and (1, 4), I'll follow these steps:

**Step 1:** Find the slope of the line joining the two points.

The slope of a line passing through two points `(x1, y1)` and `(x2, y2)` is given by:

m = (y2 - y1) / (x2 - x1)

Plugging in the values, we get:

m = (4 - 6) / (1 - 5)
= (-2) / (-4)
= 1/2

**Step 2:** Find the slope of the right bisector.

The slope of the right bisector is the negative reciprocal of the slope found in step 1. So, if the original slope is m, then the slope of the right bisector is -1/m.

In this case, the slope of the right bisector is:

-1 / (1/2)
= -2

**Step 3:** Find the equation of the right bisector using point-slope form.

The point-slope form of a line is given by:

y - y1 = m(x - x1)

where `(x1, y1)` is a point on the line and `m` is the slope.

Using one of the points, say (5, 6), and the slope found in step 2, we get:

y - 6 = -2(x - 5)

Simplifying this equation, we get:

y - 6 = -2x + 10

**Step 4:** Write the equation in the required format.

To write the equation in the required format, we'll add 6 to both sides of the equation:

y = -2x + 16

Now, I have the solution. Here it is as a JSON object:

## Step 3: Simplify the equation to find the slope in terms of H.
m = (-2)/(6 - H)

## Step 4: Set up an equation using the slope-intercept form of a line, y = mx + b, where m is the slope and b is the y-intercept.

## Step 6: Simplify the equation by multiplying both sides by (6-H).

## Step 7: Expand and simplify the equation further.

## Step 8: Rearrange the terms to isolate H on one side of the equation.

***********************To find the equation of the line that is perpendicular to the given line X - 7y + 5 = 0, we need to follow these steps:

1. Convert the given line to slope-intercept form (y = mx + c), where m is the slope.

X - 7y + 5 = 0

Rearrange the equation to isolate y:
-7y = -X + 5
y = (-X + 5) / -7
y = X/7 - 5/7

Now we have the line in slope-intercept form, so the slope (m) is 1/7.

2. The slope of a perpendicular line is the negative reciprocal of the original slope.
So, the slope of the perpendicular line is -7.

3. We are given that the x-intercept of the perpendicular line is 5. So we have:
y = -7x + c
0 = -7(5) + c
c = 35

Therefore, the equation of the perpendicular line is y = -7x + 35.

x/3 = 1

x = 3

y = 4

***********************A nice geometry problem!

Here's my solution:

First, we need to find the equation of the line in the form `y = mx + c`, where `m` is the slope and `c` is the y-intercept.

The given equation is `(x + 12) = (y-4)/24`. Let's simplify it:

`24(x + 12) = 10(y - 4)`
`24x + 288 = 10y - 40`
`10y - 24x = 328`

Now, divide both sides by `-1` and rearrange to get the equation in slope-intercept form:

`-10y + 24x = -328`
`y = (-24/10)x + 32.8`

So, the line's equation is `y = (-12/5)x + 32.8`.

Now, let's find the distance from the point `-2, 2` to this line.

The formula for the distance between a point `(x1, y1)` and a line `Ax + By + C = 0` is:

`distance = |A(x - x1) + B(y - y1)| / √(A^2 + B^2)`

In our case, `A = -12/5`, `B = 1`, `C = -32.8`, `x1 = -2`, and `y1 = 2`.

Plugging in these values:

`distance = |(-12/5)(-2) + (1)(2)| / √((-12/5)^2 + 1^2)`
`= |24/5 + 2| / √(144/25 + 1)`
`= |48/5| / √(169/25)`
`= 48/5 / 13`
`= 48/65`

So, the distance from point `-2, 2` to the line is:

`{'Final Answer' : '48/65'}`

***********************A classic linear equation problem!

To convert the given equation to intercept form, we need to isolate y.

Given equation: 6x + 4y - 24 = 0

Add 24 to both sides:
6x + 4y = 24

Subtract 6x from both sides:
4y = -6x + 24

Divide both sides by 4:
y = (-6/4)x + 24/4
y = (-3/2)x + 6

Now we have the equation in intercept form: y = mx + c, where m is the slope and c is the y-intercept.

The y-intercept is 6, which means the line intersects the y-axis at (0, 6).

To find the x-intercept, we set y = 0:
0 = (-3/2)x + 6

Subtract 6 from both sides:
-6 = (-3/2)x

Multiply both sides by -2/3:
x = 12/3
x = 4

So, the x-intercept is (4, 0).

The final answer in JSON format:

{
  'Final Answer': '(4, 0)'
}

The given equation is:

12x - 9y = 18

To convert this equation into intercept form, we need to rewrite it in the form y = mx + b, where m is the slope and b is the y-intercept.

First, let's isolate y:

-9y = 18 - 12x
y = (18 - 12x) / -9

Now, we can simplify the equation:

y = (-2x + 2) / 3

This is now in intercept form. To find the x-intercept, set y = 0 and solve for x:

0 = (-2x + 2) / 3
-2x + 2 = 0
-2x = -2
x = 1

To find the y-intercept, set x = 0 and solve for y:

y = (18 - 12(0)) / 3
y = 6

So, the intercepts are (1, 0) and (0, 6).

d = |(x1 - x2)| = |(1-2)| = |-1| = 1
এখন, d থেকে (1,2) পোটেন্টের দিকে লাইন 4x + 7y + 5 = 0-এর তির্যক দূরত্বের মধ্যে পার্থক্য গণনা করুন।

m = (-4/7)
d = | (x1 - x2)/(-4/7)| = |(1-2)/(4/7)| = |-1/(4/7)| = 7/4

এখন, d (লাইনের দিকে তির্যক) থেকে (1, 2)-পোটেন্টের দিকে দূরত্বের মধ্যে পার্থক্য (d) গণনা করুন।

d = d - 7/4
= 1 - 7/4
= 3 / 2

***********************A nice problem in geometry!

Let's break it down step by step.

The equation of the line is $4x - y = 0$. We can rewrite this as $y = 4x$, which is in slope-intercept form, with a slope of 4.

The point given is $P(4, 1)$.

To find the distance from the point to the line, we can use the formula:

$$d = \frac{|Ax + By + C|}{\sqrt{A^2 + B^2}}$$

where $(x, y)$ are the coordinates of the point, and $Ax + By + C = 0$ is the equation of the line.

In this case, we have:

$$4x - y = 0$$

So, $A = 4$, $B = -1$, and $C = 0$. Plugging in the values of $(x, y) = (4, 1)$, we get:

$$d = \frac{|4(4) - 1 + 0|}{\sqrt{4^2 + (-1)^2}}$$

Simplifying this expression, we get:

$$d = \frac{|16 - 1|}{\sqrt{17}} = \frac{15}{\sqrt{17}}$$

Now, let's find the angle between the line and the positive x-axis. We know that the slope of the line is $4$, which means it makes an angle of $\tan^{-1}(4)$ with the positive x-axis.

Since we want to find the angle in degrees, we can use a calculator to get:

$$\tan^{-1}(4) \approx 76.0^\circ$$

So, the final answer is:

{'Final Answer' : '15/\sqrt{17}'}

Note that I didn't simplify the expression inside the square root, as it's not necessary for this problem. If you want to simplify it further, we can do that too!

To find the equation of a line that is parallel to the given lines, we need to determine the slope of the given lines first. Then, we can use the slope and one point on the given line to find the equation of the new line.

Let's start by finding the slope of the two given lines:

**Line 1:** 9x + 6y - 7 = 0

To find the slope, we need to put the equation in the form y = mx + b. We can do this by solving for y:

6y = -9x + 7
y = (-9/6)x + 7/6

So, the slope of Line 1 is -9/6, which simplifies to -3/2.

**Line 2:** 3x + 2y + 6 = 0

Again, we need to put the equation in the form y = mx + b:

2y = -3x - 6
y = (-3/2)x - 3

So, the slope of Line 2 is also -3/2.

Since both lines have the same slope, they are parallel to each other. Therefore, the equation of a line that is parallel to both given lines will have the same slope, which is -3/2.

To find the equation of this new line, we can use the point-slope form: y - y1 = m(x - x1), where (x1, y1) is any point on the line. Let's choose the point (0, -3) from Line 2:

y - (-3) = (-3/2)(x - 0)

Simplifying this equation, we get:

y + 3 = (-3/2)x

Multiplying both sides by 2 to eliminate the fraction, we get:

2y + 6 = -3x

Now, we can write the equation of the new line in standard form:

-3x + 2y + 6 = 0
